## 24. The History-Identity Cost Decision: `requires_history` for the Query Profile

> **Spawned-by:** §22 (split)

Split out of §22 on 2026-08-07 because it is a measure-then-decide question, not wiring. The rest of §22 is concrete and its outcome is known before it starts: route the reader through the shared validator, stop the transports swallowing the refusal, pin the fixtures. This item's outcome is NOT known -- it may end with `requires_history=True` on the query profile, or with `corpus_history_id` being REPLACED by a cheaper identity that never walks history. Bundling an open design decision behind a `re-measure first` prerequisite into a section of otherwise-mechanical work is what turns one section into a long tail, and the two halves fail differently: §22 fails by answering from a partial cache, this one fails by making every query slow or by binding identity to something that misses a real history change.

- [x] Re-measure the history projection before turning `requires_history` on
      Measured 2026-08-08 on the current corpus (3,807 corpus-touching commits of 5,387 total): `corpus_history_id` 47.9ms median, of which `git rev-list --count HEAD -- .` alone is 44.2ms; `query.py` 169ms per invocation. Recorded in the `corpus_history_id` docstring.
      - §21 NAMED THE WRONG VARIABLE and the re-measure is what caught it: the cost grows with TOTAL repo commits, not corpus-touching ones. By walk depth: 8.2ms at 100, 14.7 at 1,000, 22.4 at 2,000, 36.1 at 4,000, 44.5 at 5,387 -- about 6.9us per commit walked plus ~7.5ms spawn -- and a pathspec matching only 44 commits still costs 25.5ms, because the expense is the tree-diff across the whole walk rather than the matches. So the ~100ms crossover arrives at 10,000 TOTAL commits, roughly 1.4x sooner than §21's model implied.
      - `git commit-graph write --changed-paths` was measured as a way out and rejected: 45.3ms -> 38.8ms (1.2x), because `--count` cannot early-exit. There is no cheap EXACT reader-side identity.
- [x] Evaluate the tip-plus-shallow-boundary alternative against that measurement
      REJECTED as unsound, with the counterexample reproduced rather than argued. Recorded in the `corpus_history_id` docstring.
      - The case the count catches and the digest does not: with `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` pointing elsewhere, `git log` and `git rev-list` both walk the overridden boundary while the on-disk shallow file stays byte-identical and `rev-parse --git-path shallow` still resolves the default. Reproduced on git 2.43 -- the count went 2 to 1 across the override with the file unchanged. The count is computed by git under the same environment as the walk; a file digest is not.
      - The case NEITHER catches: a replacement preserving tree and parents but changing the committer timestamp moves `%ct` while leaving tip and count equal. Filed as §27, not swallowed.
      - It also breaks §21's single-walk provenance rule: `build.py:289` derives BOTH halves from one `git log`, so a separately-read digest reopens the A-to-B-to-A window that rule closed.
- [x] Pin the chosen identity source for the query profile, with the reasoning recorded
      The identity is UNCHANGED (`tip:count`, walk-derived) and the axis moved from the READER to the VERB: added `PROFILE_QUERY_HISTORY` (`cache_schema.py`, same five subtrees, `requires_history=True`), selected per verb by `_profile_for` in `query.py`.
      - Three of thirteen commands dereference a git-derived timestamp: `stale` (`query.py:893`), `stats` (`:1009`, `:1028`, `:1034`) and `render --render-format gantt` (`render.py:375`). The other ten stay on `PROFILE_QUERY` and pay nothing.
      - Measured cost of the decision, AFTER the review round collapsed the two history probes into one and stopped the bounded read asking for the 16 MiB ceiling per file: history verbs 202-204ms, the ten blind verbs 150ms -- against a 169ms pre-section baseline, so the common path got FASTER while the three consumers gained the guarantee. Reader-wide enablement would instead have put ~+50ms on all thirteen to protect fields ten of them never read.
      - The §22 docstring claim that `query.py` sorts "stale / deferred" by `last_active_at` was WRONG about `deferred`, which dereferences no timestamp; corrected in place.
- [x] Resolve the double-hash of the corpus per process, or record why it stays
      IT STAYS, and the numbers are corrected. The second pass is not a redundant hash of the same bytes -- it is a second OBSERVATION at a different time, and it is the whole mechanism catching a corpus edit that lands mid-walk. Cheapening it with a stat/mtime pre-filter would reintroduce the clock proxy §21 removed.
      - Measured 27.8ms per pass warm over 232 files / 10.83 MB, so ~56ms for the pair; the recorded "9.5ms per pass" was 3x optimistic on an essentially unchanged corpus. Within one pass: ~7ms walk, 13.7ms `read_corpus_file` against 4.3ms for a plain read, 6.7ms sha256 -- the bounded-read wrapper is the larger term, not the hashing.
      - §21's "5% crossover at a 30-70 MB corpus" was anchored to `validate.py`'s ~1s symbol walk, where it holds at ~5.6%. `query.py` has no such baseline: at 169ms the pair is ALREADY ~33%, so that crossover was passed on the query path before it was written down.
- [x] Fixtures for whichever identity ships, each mutation-checked
      Twelve fixtures in `tests/test_build.sh` (431/431 green), including a control asserting the fixture tree actually built a cache -- two earlier drafts of this probe were vacuous because an invalid frontmatter id made the build fail and every profile then read as "refused".
      - Mutations verified to FAIL the fixtures, not asserted: making the history check unconditional flips the discrimination fixture from `STALE ACCEPT` to `STALE STALE`; neutering `check_history_unchanged` flips the post-walk fixture from `STALE` to `MISSED`.
      - The deepening fixture the original text called for is NOT here, because the identity did not change: reverting to the tip alone is a mutation against §21's shipped count, which §27 owns.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: pin the query profile's history identity and its cost"`

**Test checkpoint:** the query profile's identity source is pinned with a recorded measurement rather than left unset; a byte-identical corpus over a rewritten history is refused for the history-declaring profile and invisible to the blind one; the per-invocation cost on the query path is measured on the current corpus and stated; each fixture fails when its mechanism is reverted; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `PROFILE_QUERY_HISTORY` + per-verb selection in `query.py`, `CacheInfo.history`, `check_history_unchanged`, and typed provenance markers in `build.py`; 12 fixtures, 431/431 in `test_build.sh`.
> - The identity itself is UNCHANGED: the measurement rejected the cheaper alternative rather than adopting it, so this section pins and documents `tip:count` instead of replacing it.
> - Downstream: the three timestamp-consuming verbs cost 202-204ms and the other ten 150ms, against a 169ms pre-section baseline -- the common path got faster; `build.py` also refuses to publish timestamps it did not derive.
> - Canonical doc is the `corpus_history_id` and `_scan_corpus` docstrings in `cache_schema.py` -- both carry the corrected §21 measurements and the named coverage limits.
> - Scope boundary: the replace-ref/graft axis is §27 (shipped) and watch-mode history triggering is §30, not here.

Also `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (`1293/1293`). Host-side tooling; no kernel `TEST_CAT_*` surface and no `scripts/debug/kernel/run-*.bat`.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-08 | commit `277c39584` | 6/6 items | build OK | test_build 432/432, tooling 1293/1293, kernel 28326+17, lint 0 errors, SMOKE TEST PASSED
> **Deferred:** [M] CLOSED 2026-08-09 by §27 (commit `f814af76f`): replacements and grafts are now inert on both sides via `HISTORY_GIT_GLOBALS` + `history_git_env()` (RESOLVED 2026-08-13 in §27, commit `f814af76f`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §27 (item: "REJECT replacement ancestry -- `cache_schema.HISTORY_GIT_GLOBALS = (\"--no-replace-objects\",)` on every git call in both `collect_git_timestamps` and `corpus_history_id`" at line 1378)

## 25. Fields the Readers Consume That the Producer Never Emits

> **Spawned-by:** §22 (review)

Found on 2026-08-07 while routing `query.py` through the shared validator (§22): building the profile required inventorying every field the readers actually dereference, and two of them are read by code that can never receive them. Neither is a crash -- both degrade to a silent default, which is why neither surfaced. Filed as its own section because both candidate owners (§4 query CLI, §7 visualization) carry Verified and Quality-reviewed stamps, so a bare item in either is invisible to every later pass.

- [x] `effort` is carried end to end, behind a fail-closed duration grammar
      The field now flows frontmatter -> producer -> cache -> Gantt row. `cache_schema.EFFORT_REGEX` is the ONE grammar, shared by `build.py`'s frontmatter check and copy list, `cache.schema.json`, `_validate_node_fields`, and `render._gantt_effort` as the last guard.
      - Direction chosen: CARRY, not delete -- deleting the read keeps today's misleading uniform schedule and drops a capability §7 shipped.
      - `\A[1-9][0-9]*[dw]\Z`, narrower than Mermaid's grammar on purpose. A present-but-invalid value FAILS the build rather than defaulting; a silent default hides a typo behind a plausible schedule.
      - `\Z` not `$`, measured rather than reasoned: Python's `$` matches before a trailing newline, so the first draft ACCEPTED `"2w\n"` -- the exact injection the guard exists to stop. Caught by the probe's own control.
      - §7's Notes claimed no live TODO carried it "yet", reading as a usage gap a contributor could close. They could not; that line is corrected.
- [x] File-level `depends_on` is pinned as the ONE readiness source, and its emptiness is now visible
      Direction chosen: keep the verbs frontmatter-only and SAY so. `query.py` prints a stderr note whenever the edge set is empty, so an unconditionally-empty `blocked` / `blocking` can no longer read as "nothing is blocked"; `--quiet` and stdout are untouched.
      - Redirecting them at `sections[].depends_on` was rejected on measurement, not taste: of 2,529 live groups (the earlier 2,527 was stale), **1,806 target `self`**, so a derivation either self-depends or widens a section dependency into whole-file completion.
      - The decisive one: the two fields are different NAMESPACES -- frontmatter holds TODO ids, section groups hold unqualified stems, and **26 of 145 cross-file targets match more than one file** (`TODO-01` matches 18). An unresolved-reference problem, owned by §26.
      - Section-aware verbs therefore belong on top of §26 as NEW commands with stated aggregation rules; reinterpreting `ready` is the one option that changes an existing answer silently.
- [x] The cache-format identity ships in the corpus binding, and a freshness-disabled read REQUIRES it
      `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` + `PRODUCER_CONTRACT_DIGEST` ride in the binding; `check_cache_format` verifies them before any node walk and refuses with the new `REASON_LEGACY_FORMAT`, so `--diff`'s refusal is a CHECK, not a request.
      - Two values because one cannot do the job: the integer catches a field that changed MEANING, the digest a structural change when the bump is forgotten. Taken over the DECLARATION (`EMITTED_NODE_FIELDS`), never an observed cache; `build.py` refuses an undeclared key, so the two cannot drift.
      - A ROOT ENVELOPE was designed first and REJECTED: the cache root is a bare array parsed directly by control-plane tools this run may not rewrite, and JSON has no both-object-and-array shape. The digest-named sidecar binds the same bytes.
      - Requiring a binding is DERIVED from `check_stale`, not declared per caller: the per-caller flag let an unidentified cache reach every renderer (Codex adversarial). A refused baseline is left byte-identical.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: close the read-but-never-emitted field gaps"`

**Test checkpoint:** met -- `effort` round-trips frontmatter to Gantt row and every hostile value refuses the build (including the trailing-newline case a `$`-anchored regex accepts); the readiness verbs name their dependency source and go quiet once an edge exists; a baseline carrying an older contract stamp is REFUSED by `--diff` with its bytes intact, and a same-version digest drift refuses with a different message. `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §7`](#7-visualization-output-mermaid--graphviz--ascii--gantt--markdown) -- the section whose Gantt emitter makes the dead `effort` read and whose Notes state the reason incorrectly (item: "Commit: `\"todo-graph: visualization output (mermaid/dot/ascii/gantt/markdown)\"`"); that section is stamped, so the correction is owned here.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §22`](#22-querypy-fail-closed-through-every-transport) -- the section that found both while inventorying what `query.py` consumes (item: "Route `query.py` through the shared validator, fail-closed"); §22 made the reads SAFE, and deliberately did not change which fields the producer populates.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §23`](#23-validatepy-rebuild-recovery-and-the-baseline-cache) -- the section that needs the cache-format identity and shipped without it (item: "Give the `--diff` baseline cache its own profile"); its baseline refusal could only ASK for a current-producer artifact, and now checks one: `--diff` passes `require_format_binding=True` and refuses an unidentified baseline at rc 2 with its bytes intact.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §26`](#26-domain-code-resolution-picks-a-directory-by-cache-order) -- the section that owns qualified target resolution (item: "Commit: `\"todo-graph: domain codes resolve by directory, not by cache order\"`"); section-level dependency targets are unqualified stems, 26 of 145 matching more than one file, which is why this section pinned the readiness verbs to the frontmatter key instead of redirecting them.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` | expected: `[test_build] 460/460 passed`, 0 failed -- the twenty-eight section-25 fixtures (effort round trip + hostile values + schema/Python grammar agreement, the dependency-source note and its conditionality, the old-contract and drifted-digest baseline refusals, byte-identical baseline, undeclared-key producer bound, sidecar wire-format gate, standalone present-null Gantt guard, declaration-vs-schema agreement, the lint reason-to-rc completeness assert, frontmatter-schema-vs-producer agreement, the caveat surviving `--quiet` on both the bounded and `stats` paths, a FIFO binding refused rather than hanging, one binding generation shared by the format and freshness checks, the cache-only contract, both schemas refusing a trailing-newline `effort` through `Draft202012Validator`, the bidirectional producer-declaration bound, and identity-before-shape on an ABSENT binding) and their nine mutation controls. Also `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (`1319/1319`). Host-side tooling; no kernel `TEST_CAT_*` surface and no `scripts/debug/kernel/run-*.bat`.

> **Notes:**
> `effort` now flows frontmatter -> producer -> cache -> Gantt row behind one shared grammar (`cache_schema.EFFORT_REGEX`), fail-closed: an invalid value refuses the build instead of silently rendering the `1w` default.
> The cache-format identity (`cache_format_version` + `producer_contract_digest`) rides in the corpus binding, is verified before any subtree walk, and is REQUIRED by `validate.py --diff`, whose baseline refusal is now a check rather than a request.
> A root envelope was designed and rejected on measurement: the cache root is parsed directly by control-plane tools this run may not rewrite, and the digest-named sidecar binds to the same bytes.
> `ready` / `blocked` / `blocking` stay frontmatter-only by decision, and now NAME their empty edge set on stderr so an unconditionally-empty ranking cannot read as a verdict; `--quiet` and stdout are unchanged.
> Canonical doc: `docs/infrastructure/todo-metadata.md` ("Optional Fields", "Which dependency evidence the readiness verbs consult", "The Corpus Binding").
> Scope boundary: section-aware readiness verbs are NOT shipped and are not a variant of these three -- they need §26's qualified resolution first; `SIDECAR_SCHEMA` is deliberately NOT bumped, because an old reader parses the added keys harmlessly and a new one refuses an old binding by their absence.
> Review (2026-08-09) changed three things the ship had wrong, none of them cosmetic. IDENTITY BEFORE SHAPE did not hold for an ABSENT binding: `check_cache_format` returned quietly under freshness-ON and left the refusal to `check_freshness`, which runs AFTER `validate_nodes` -- so an unbound cache that also violated a subtree was reported as SHAPE, naming the wrong defect. It now refuses before the walk, as `REASON_LEGACY_FORMAT` for both callers; refusing as STALE was tried first and reverted, because STALE is absent from `validate.py:_REBUILDABLE_REASONS` and turned a recoverable legacy cache into a hard stop.
> The producer contract was checked in ONE direction only -- `keys - EMITTED_NODE_FIELDS` cannot see a field that stopped being emitted, and the `emission-drop` guard iterated the same list the copy loop did, so deleting a field removed it from both. The producer now declares `_ALWAYS_EMITTED` / `_CONDITIONAL_EMITTED`, asserts their union against the shared declaration at import, checks every unconditional key per node, and -- because the union FLATTENS the emission mode and a category swap defeats it -- cross-checks the always-set against `cache.schema.json`'s `required` list, which was a byte-equal hand-written duplicate and is now the single authority. The conditional `stamped_items` emission is guarded separately: dropping it is fail-OPEN, not lossy, because an all-absent population reads as legacy and `lint.sh:574` downgrades Check 7 to a warning.
> Both published schemas gained `not: {pattern: "[\\r\\n]"}` on `effort`. JSON Schema pins no regex engine and Python's `re` matches `$` before a trailing newline, so the anchored pattern accepted `"2w\n"` through `Draft202012Validator` -- the exact value `EFFORT_REGEX`'s `\Z` exists to refuse. The fixture that should have caught it was hand-reimplementing the validator with a `match.end()` test and now routes through `Draft202012Validator` itself.
> `PRODUCER_CONTRACT_DIGEST` MOVED ONCE as a result (modes are now hashed with the names). No digest literal is pinned anywhere in the tree, and `REASON_LEGACY_FORMAT` is rebuildable, so every existing binding self-heals through the ordinary rebuild path rather than needing a migration.
> Reviewer-to-automation: `abi-mismatch` and `missing-error-path` are both past the finding-ledger promotion threshold, and this section IS that promotion for the declaration-drift class -- the checks are import-time asserts and per-node scans in the producer, not review findings, so the whole class is refused before a cache is ever written.

> **Verified:** 2026-08-09 | commit `c2c9015af` | 4/4 items | build OK | test_build 460/460, tooling 1319/1319, kernel 28326+17 user-mode, lint 0 errors
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-09 | Codex 8x (adversarial, consistency, perf, re-adversarial x5) | 7M fixed, 0 open | scope: N/A (host tooling -- no kernel/boot/desktop/shell/userland surface)

## 26. Domain-Code Resolution Picks a Directory by Cache Order

Found on 2026-08-07 by §22's round-7 review, in the one `resolve_xref_target` branch that survived that section's collision sweep. Both domain-only branches answer by SCANNING `by_dn` for the first entry whose two-digit prefix matches, so they return an arbitrary member whenever a code maps to more than one directory. §22 closed every other arbitrary-member path in the resolver; this one needs a new index rather than a guard, which is why it is filed instead of fixed there.

- [x] Index domain CODES to their full set of parent directories, and resolve every directory-qualified form through the DIRECTORY
      `validate.py` resolves `D14` and a named domain path by iterating `path_index["by_dn"]` and returning the first `fp` whose `d == dom`. With `todo/14-alpha/TODO-01-a.md` and `todo/14-beta/TODO-02-b.md` both present, `D14` resolves to alpha or beta purely by node order -- and the EXPLICIT token `14-alpha` resolves to beta when beta is inserted first, which is a wrong binding rather than a coin flip.
      - §22's `_validate_resolver_keys_unique` does NOT catch this: the `(domain, number)` keys differ (`('14',1)` vs `('14',2)`), so the cache is valid. The collision is in the code-to-DIRECTORY mapping, which nothing indexes today.
      - Fix direction: build `{domain_code: {parent_dirs}}` alongside `by_dn`. A named domain path must match its parent directory exactly; compact `D14` resolves only when the code owns exactly one directory, and otherwise returns None.
      - The live corpus has one directory per code, so this refuses nothing today -- it removes a silent wrong answer that appears the moment a second directory shares a code.
      - §22's review reproduced it concretely: with `todo/01-a/TODO-01-a.md` and `todo/01-b/TODO-02-b.md`, resolving the EXPLICIT target `01-b/INDEX.md` returns `todo/01-a/INDEX.md`, and reversing cache order reverses the answer. `_validate_resolver_keys_unique` cannot catch it -- the `(domain, number)` keys differ, so the cache is valid.
      - SHIPPED WIDER THAN FILED, because the review found the same substitution in five more branches. `build_path_index` now emits `by_dirnum {(dir,num): path}`, `by_code {code: {dirs}}` and `dir_paths {dir: parent}` with their own collision sets; `D<code>` resolves only when the code owns exactly one directory; `T<n>` and bare `TODO-<n>` resolve against the source file's own DIRECTORY rather than its two digits; and the letter branch qualifies by directory too.
      - A SPELLED FILENAME IS DECIDED BY ITS SLUG, with no number fallback -- the rule §22 already applied to spelled letter targets. Branches 4a/5a and the relative form reduced `TODO-05-win32-file-io-api.md` to `(dir, 5)`, so it was answered by `TODO-05-object-manager.md`. Measured old-vs-new over all 4,480 live targets: 6 rebound, every one from a wrong file to the file it names; 0 lost, 0 gained.
      - The corpus debt this exposed was repaired in two separate `todo:` commits (188 pre-renumber directory names; 8 references naming files that do not exist), each green under the OLD resolver so neither is entangled with this change.
- [x] Precompute the path set the resolver rebuilds per reference
      `_all_paths` (`validate.py:315-328`) walks every indexed path to build a fresh set on each call, and `resolve_xref_target` calls it for relative/path-like references once per graph edge, turning those cases from O(E) into O(E*V). Owned here because this section is already rebuilding that index, so the set lands beside `by_dn` rather than being bolted on afterwards.
      - Measured 2026-08-08: only ~5-6ms today (78 of 2,895 resolution calls take the path), but a synthetic path-heavy corpus went 45.9ms at 500 nodes to 1.815s at 4,000 -- 39.5x for 8x the input. It is a scaling cliff, not a current cost.
      - Compute it ONCE in `build_path_index` and store it in `path_index`, so every membership test is O(1) and no caller can reintroduce the rebuild.
      - Shipped as a `frozenset` under `all_paths`, read through the existing `_all_paths` accessor so a hand-built index still answers. Measured old vs new: 29.3ms -> 9.9ms at 500 nodes and 1604.5ms -> 78.5ms at 4,000. The old curve grows 55x for 8x the input, the new one 7.9x -- the cliff is gone, not merely shifted.
- [x] Fixtures in BOTH node orders, each mutation-checked
      Order-independence is the property under test, so every case runs with the nodes forward and reversed: `D14` with two directories refuses, `14-alpha` resolves to alpha regardless of insertion order, and a single-directory code still resolves both forms. Mutation: restore the first-match scan and require the reversed-order case to FAIL.
      - Shipped as sub-tests 9aa16b/9aa16c in `scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`: 16 cases x 2 orders, plus a single-directory set proving the live corpus shape is untouched (a fix that refused everything would pass the order assertions), letter-numbered and letter-only-sibling cases, spelled-stem cases, and both directions of the hand-built-index fallback.
      - The mutation restores the first-match scan on the live module and asserts the two orders DISAGREE, so the fixture fails if the fix is reverted. 464/464 `test_build.sh`, 1319/1319 `test-tooling.sh`.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: domain codes resolve by directory, not by cache order"`

**Test checkpoint:** a domain code owning two directories refuses the compact form and resolves each named form to its own directory, in both node orders; a single-directory code is unaffected; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (sub-tests 9aa16b/9aa16c) -- expect 464/464; full gate `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` expect 1319/1319.

> **Notes:**
> - `build_path_index` keys directories directly (`by_dirnum`, `by_code`, `dir_paths`, each with a collision set) and precomputes `all_paths` as a frozenset.
> - Six resolver branches substituted a two-digit code for a directory, not the three filed here; three were flatly wrong rather than order-dependent.
> - A spelled filename now resolves by its exact stem with no number fallback -- the rule §22 already applied to spelled letter targets.
> - Downstream: `docs/infrastructure/todo-graph.md` regenerates with four corrected edges. Over all 4,480 live targets: 6 rebound, 0 lost, 0 gained.
> - The corpus debt this exposed landed as two separate `todo:` commits, each verified green under the pre-change resolver.
> - Scope boundary: no new reference form is accepted, and `by_dn` keeps its code-keyed answer for genuinely code-qualified `D<dd>T<nn>`.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-09 | commit `0c5928fe6` | 4/4 items | build OK | test_build 464/464, test-tooling 1319/1319, corpus 9/9 checks 0 failures
> **Accepted:** [H] stamp-target capture splits a space-bearing markdown label, so a legitimate label cannot resolve (reason: producer-side, `build.py` not the resolver; §26 closed the dangerous half by failing closed) (RESOLVED 2026-08-13 in §33, commit `c6af1c6a6`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §33 (item: "Capture the complete markdown link as one target, not `\S+`" at line 1706)

## 27. Effective-History Identity: Replace Refs and Grafts

> **Spawned-by:** §24 (review)

Found by §24's reviews on 2026-08-08, while §24 was pinning what the query profile's identity is derived from. §24 measured the identity's COST, pinned its consumers and enabled the axis for the three commands that read git-derived timestamps; this section carries what the identity cannot SEE on that same axis. Filed separately because §21 owns `corpus_history_id` and is stamped, and because the repair is its own machinery rather than the profile wiring §24 changed.

**Split 2026-08-09, before implementation.** §24's residue named two gaps on the history axis and the section-manifest complexity predictor flagged the pair `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED`. They share a cause and nothing else: this one is about what the identity PROJECTION discards (git ancestry probing in `cache_schema.corpus_history_id` + `build.collect_git_timestamps`, decided by a choice between refusing and folding), while the other is about a WATCHER that never re-asks (the event loop in `query.watch_loop`, decided by a polling-cost budget). Different files, different failure modes -- a wrong answer versus a stale one -- and different tests. The watch half is now §30.

`corpus_history_id` (`cache_schema.py:1491-1500`) projects the effective history onto two values: the latest corpus-touching commit hash and the count of corpus-touching commits. Those two can collide across genuinely different effective histories. Replace an OLDER corpus-touching commit with one carrying an identical tree and identical parents but a different committer timestamp, and `git log` hands `build.py:269` a different `%ct` -- so `created_at` / `last_active_at` change -- while the latest touching hash and the commit count both stay equal. An equal-depth graft has the same shape. The cache is then certified against a history it did not come from.

This is NOT the shallow-deepening case, which the count does catch because deepening changes the cardinality. It is specifically the class where ancestry semantics change while cardinality and tip do not. The `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` case splits across that line rather than sitting on one side of it: a boundary move that changes the count is caught, and an EQUAL-COUNT boundary move is not -- reproduced during this section's review and filed as §31, because the count following the effective git environment is not the same property as the count always moving.

- [x] REJECT replacement ancestry -- `cache_schema.HISTORY_GIT_GLOBALS = ("--no-replace-objects",)` on every git call in both `collect_git_timestamps` and `corpus_history_id`
      Folding was rejected on evidence, not preference: `git replace` honours `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE`, so a digest of `refs/replace` reads an EMPTY set while the walk is replaced.
      - Measured 2026-08-09 on git 2.43.0 with a control: a replace ref on the oldest corpus commit (identical tree and parents, new committer date) left tip AND count unchanged while `%ct` went 1577836800 -> 1622959566; `--no-replace-objects` restored it, including under a custom ref base.
      - The flag rides on EVERY probe in `corpus_history_id._git`, not only the two that walk history, so the next probe added there inherits the contract instead of having to remember it.
- [x] Grafts are inert BY CONSTRUCTION via `cache_schema.history_git_env()` (`GIT_GRAFT_FILE=os.devnull`), not by a presence probe
      A probe was designed first and rejected: it is a SECOND read not causally bound to the traversal, so a graft can be live for the producer's walk and gone before the reader stats it (Codex design review, [high]).
      - `--no-replace-objects` does not disable grafts and git offers no flag; an EMPTY graft file is a no-op, so pointing `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` at the null device is the exact equivalent. Verified: an ancestry-changing graft moved the count 3 -> 2 and dropped a commit from the walk; both restored with the env set.
      - This ignores a graft rather than refusing it, which the original item allowed only if not done SILENTLY -- so the contract is stated in the `corpus_history_id` docstring and in the module comment, not left implicit.
- [x] `corpus_history_id`'s documented guarantee narrowed to the replacement- and graft-neutralised history, and two FALSE clauses removed
      The term is deliberately not "raw": shallow ancestry stays EFFECTIVE on both sides, so "raw" would name a stronger contract than the code delivers.
      - §24's wording claimed the pair covered "boundary changes however they are reached". Verified false 2026-08-09: with C0 adding a TODO, C1/C2 touching nothing under the corpus and C3 touching it, a `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` boundary at C2 leaves tip AND count identical (2) while `created_at` moves 1577836800 -> 1583020800.
      - The second false clause said the COUNT was the only half following the effective git environment. The tip follows it too: verified with C0 touching the corpus and C1 not, a boundary at C1 moved the tip from C0 to C1 with the count unchanged at 1.
      - The docstring now states both limits and names §31 as the owner of the open axis, rather than implying it is covered. (§31 SHIPPED 2026-08-09 and closed that axis by refusing shallow corpora, so the docstring no longer names an open owner; the two limits above remain stated as history.)
- [x] `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` 1 -> 2, because `history_id` kept its name and type and changed its meaning
      This is the documented purpose of the hand-bumped integer (`cache_schema.py:136`): a v1 `tip:count` was computed with replacements and grafts APPLIED, a v2 one with both neutralised, and the two strings compare EQUAL in exactly the replacement case that matters.
      - Without it a v2 reader accepts a v1 cache built under a replacement, and a v1 reader accepts a v2 cache after a rollback -- both silently, since no digest can see a meaning change (Codex adversarial, [high]).
- [x] Fixtures in `tests/test_build.sh`, every leg controlled and mutation-checked
      27a replace ref, 27b the same under `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE=refs/myreplace/`, 27c an ancestry-changing graft; each asserts the (tip, count) pair is UNCHANGED first, so none of them can pass on §21's count check instead.
      - Mutation legs revert each mechanism and require the drift to APPEAR. Both initially reported a false pass because a two-file module copy died on a missing `schema/`, so "no drift" and "no build" now report as distinct outcomes.
      - 27b additionally proves an UNPROTECTED build drifts under the custom base before asserting the protected one is stable, and records every git setup command's exit status (Codex adversarial, [medium]).
      - Repaired an unrelated fixture the bump exposed: 22am hardcoded a `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION = 1` needle, so a legitimate bump silently made base and head identical and failed BOTH gate cases as "not handled". It now bumps whatever value it finds.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: close the replace-ref and graft axis in the history identity"`

**Test checkpoint:** a replace ref that alters a corpus-touching commit's timestamp without moving the tip or the count is either refused or detected, and the fixture proving it fails when the mechanism is reverted; an ordinary repository with no replace refs and no graft file is unaffected and pays no measurable extra cost; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- expect 466/466 passed, including the two section-27 lines (the three controlled cases, and the mutation check that reverting either mechanism lets the history move the timestamps).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `cache_schema.HISTORY_GIT_GLOBALS` + `history_git_env()` and routed both git call sites through them (`build.collect_git_timestamps`, `corpus_history_id._git`), so replace refs and grafts are inert for producer and reader alike.
> - Chose rejection over folding on measured evidence: `GIT_REPLACE_REF_BASE` makes a `refs/replace` digest read empty while the walk is replaced, so candidate B could not see what it was built to see.
> - Disabling rather than probing is the load-bearing choice -- a probe is a second read that can observe a different instant than the walk, which is the ABA shape §21 already paid for.
> - `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` 1 -> 2 because `history_id` changed meaning at an unchanged name and type; every cache rebuilds, which is free for a gitignored artifact.
> - Canonical contract: the `corpus_history_id` docstring plus the module comment above `HISTORY_GIT_GLOBALS`, both of which state what is covered and what is not.
> - Scope boundary: the equal-count shallow-boundary collision is REPRODUCED but deliberately NOT closed here -- it needs a product decision about shallow clones and is filed as §31.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-09 | commit `f814af76f` | 6/6 items | build OK | test_build 466/466, tooling 1319/1319, kernel 28326+17, lint 0 errors, todo-graph 9/9
> **Deferred:** [M] CLOSED 2026-08-09 by §31: an equal-count shallow boundary moved `created_at` while tip and count compared equal; the product decision it was waiting on came down on REFUSING a shallow corpus outright (`--is-shallow-repository` before the walk, ~1.0ms), after binding was reproduced colliding on a multi-root history (RESOLVED 2026-08-13 in §31, commit `d5a695833`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §31 (item: "Decide what a shallow corpus IS to this tool, and record the reasoning")
> **Deferred:** [L] `validate.py` never checks in-file `](#...)` anchors, so a retitled heading silently breaks every inbound link (reason: a validator capability, not this section's axis; 3 of 63 links in this file were dead and are repaired in the review commit) (RESOLVED 2026-08-13 in §32, commit `b4f26f6d0`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §32 (item: "Validate IN-FILE `](#anchor)` links against the file's own headings" at line 1666)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-09 | Codex 6x (design, adversarial x3, consistency, perf) | 6H+5M+0L fixed, 2 open | scope: N/A (host tooling -- no kernel/boot/desktop/shell/userland surface)

-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §30`](#30---watch-never-ticks-on-a-history-change-for-the-history-consuming-verbs) -- the watch-mode half of §24's residue, split out of this section before implementation (item: "Give `--watch` a history trigger for the history-consuming verbs"); it consumes whatever probe this section settles on, so it ships after this one.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §24`](#24-the-history-identity-cost-decision-requires_history-for-the-query-profile) -- the section that found this and pinned the identity everywhere else (item: "Pin the chosen identity source for the query profile, with the reasoning recorded"); that section measured the cost and enabled the history axis for the three consumers, and deliberately did NOT close this gap.

SHIPPED 2026-08-09: refusal won, and binding turned out to be unavailable rather than merely unchosen -- `tip:count:oldest` collides on a multi-root history.

## 28. Section-Level Readiness: Verbs That Consult the Dependency Evidence the Graph Actually Has

> **Spawned-by:** §25 (review)

Filed by §25 on 2026-08-08, which pinned `ready` / `blocked` / `blocking` to the file-level `depends_on` frontmatter key and made their empty edge set visible rather than silent. That closed the DISHONESTY. It did not close the CAPABILITY gap, and this section exists so that distinction does not quietly become permanent: the corpus carries 723 cross-file section dependency groups that no verb consults, so "what should I work on next?" -- the headline claim of §4 and of this file's OS Comparison table -- is answered today by listing draft TODOs, and `blocking` returns 0 rows for every possible corpus.

The reason this is NOT a small change, and why §25 refused to make it: the two dependency vocabularies are different namespaces. Frontmatter `depends_on` holds TODO ids; `sections[].depends_on` holds unqualified stems, and 26 of its 145 distinct cross-file targets match more than one file (`TODO-01` matches 18, one per domain). Resolving those is §26's work, and it must land first.

- [x] Decide the aggregation rule BEFORE any code, and write it down where a reader will find it
      Pinned in prose at [Section-level readiness: the aggregation rule](../../docs/infrastructure/todo-metadata.md#section-level-readiness-the-aggregation-rule) before any code was written, and restated at the head of the implementation block in `query.py`.
      - The `self` groups are excluded from the cross-file edge set at construction, not filtered late -- but they are still evaluated, because a section whose own prerequisite is open is not workable. Ranking on cross-file edges alone gave 33 ready rows, 20 with an unmet same-file prerequisite.
      - SATISFACTION USES THE CANONICAL LIFECYCLE CLASSIFIER, NOT THE RAW `[x]`/`[/]` MARKER. The design review forced this: 42 live sections carry a raw `[x]` the oracle calls NEEDS_WORK and 421 carry a raw `[/]` it calls DONE, so the marker would disagree with the runner on 463 sections. `query.py` imports `classify_section` rather than re-deriving it. A source must classify NEEDS_WORK to be `ready` -- an `awaiting-*` park is BLOCKED, not runnable.
      - An empty section list means the whole target FILE, and only there is a file-level verdict used. A token resolving to a domain `INDEX.md` (8 live groups) is its own `non-node` reason, never satisfied and never a crash. A target resolving back to its OWN file is routed to `self` whatever it was called (8 live groups), and a row with a null section number keeps a distinct ordinal identity rather than merging with its twin.
- [x] Ship it as NEW verbs, never by reinterpreting `ready` / `blocked` / `blocking`
      `section-ready` / `section-blocked` / `section-blocking` in `query.py`, mirrored as MCP tools (15 registered, was 12). The existing three are untouched and still return exactly what they did; fixture 28h pins that.
      - Every row carries a `dep_source` column naming the graph it came from, so a caller can tell which graph produced it without reading stderr.
- [x] Retire §25's empty-edge-set note only when it stops being true
      IT IS STILL TRUE, so the note STAYS -- 0 of 232 TODOs author the file-level field, and the new verbs consult a different graph rather than making the old one non-empty. Retiring it here would have been the easy wrong move. It now names `section-ready` / `section-blocked` / `section-blocking` as the verbs to use instead.
- [x] Update the OS Comparison "Critical-path / most-blocking rank" row, which §25 downgraded to a caveat
- [x] Fixtures, each mutation-checked, over a corpus with genuinely ambiguous targets
      35 assertions in `scripts/todo-graph/tests/section28_fixtures.py`, driven by `test_build.sh` sub-test 28, plus a live-corpus check. Synthetic corpus because the live one has zero `unresolved` and zero `dangling` edges -- the two states that must not read as satisfied. `test_build.sh` pins the exact expected `SUMMARY 35 / 35`, so deleting a fixture FAILS rather than quietly shrinking coverage.
      - Eight mutation controls, each verified to FIRE: reverting to the raw marker, ignoring `self` prerequisites, dropping either dedup layer, accepting any non-DONE source, ignoring self-aliases, keying null rows by number alone, and dropping the empty self-alias. The first dedup control PASSED unchanged and was rewritten -- the dedup was layered three deep and the clause under test was not the one being reverted, so the redundant third layer was removed rather than left unfalsifiable.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: section-level readiness verbs over the resolved dependency graph"`

**Test checkpoint:** a corpus whose only dependency evidence is section-level produces a non-empty readiness answer from the new verbs and an unchanged answer from the existing three; an ambiguous bare-stem target is refused rather than bound to an arbitrary file; reverting the aggregation rule makes the fixture fail; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- expect 502/502 passed, including sub-test 28 (35 assertions from `tests/section28_fixtures.py`, 8 of them mutation controls, plus the live-corpus check and the MCP wire test that CALLS each new verb). The harness pins the exact `SUMMARY 35 / 35`, so deleting a fixture fails rather than quietly shrinking coverage.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped `section-ready` / `section-blocked` / `section-blocking` in `query.py` over `sections[].depends_on`, mirrored as MCP tools (12 -> 15). The file-level trio is untouched and still answers what it always answered.
> - Satisfaction reuses `sequencer_triage.classify_section` instead of the raw `[x]`/`[/]` marker, which disagrees with the oracle on 463 live sections. The index is lazy per-`Ctx`, so file-level verbs pay none of its cost.
> - No cache-schema change and no `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` bump: aggregation happens at query time over fields `build.py` already emits.
> - Canonical contract: [Section-level readiness: the aggregation rule](../../docs/infrastructure/todo-metadata.md#section-level-readiness-the-aggregation-rule), pinned in prose before any code; every clause there has a mutation control behind it.
> - Three defects came from the corpus, not from theory: 8 groups name their own file by code, 8 resolve to a domain `INDEX.md`, one names the same target section twice.
> - Scope boundary: perf was measured (112.3 ms index build, 219.1 ms MCP query, near-linear to 4,000 nodes) and deliberately left alone.

## 29. `--fix-line-numbers` Reports Success Over Targets It Could Not Resolve

> **Spawned-by:** §26 (review)

Found by §26's round-6 review while that section was tightening the resolver. Two properties of repair mode are independent of the resolver and were not introduced by §26, so they are filed here rather than folded into it: the repair counts unresolvable targets and then discards the count, and it will operate on a graph whose resolver keys collide. Both are pre-existing; §26 only made them legible by making the resolver refuse rather than guess.

Implementation found a THIRD case of the same defect and corrected one premise. The third: a target that resolves to a real file which does not contain the named item was skipped in silence, so fixing only the unresolvable branch would have left this section's title true. The premise: the collision case was filed as defence in depth because `cache_schema` was believed to refuse such a cache at load -- it does not, for `validate.py`'s profile. §26's resolver is what makes it safe; this section makes it legible.

- [x] The exit contract is stated once, and three incomplete-repair classes now reach it
      CONTRACT CHOSEN: rc 0 = every stamp examined is correct; rc 1 = the run RAN and is INCOMPLETE (safe rewrites still applied -- a report, not a rollback); rc 2 = REFUSED before touching anything. Recorded in the CLI header block of [`validate.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/validate.py) where `--fix-line-numbers` is documented, and again at the `return` site.
      - `main()` dropped the count as `_unres`; it now unpacks `unres` and `missing` and returns 1 when `ambig + unres + missing > 0`. WARNING was rejected as the contract: the operator asked for stamp line numbers to be made true, and a stale one that survives means the run did not do that.
      - A SECOND silent path was found by the design review and fixed with it: a target that RESOLVED but did not contain the named item hit `len(hits) == 0` and `continue`d without counter or message, so fixing only the unresolvable branch would have left the section's own title true. Counted separately as `missing_items`.
      - Each unrepaired stamp is now NAMED on its own FAIL line, not merely counted. The count reached only the summary, which was gated on `not quiet` -- and the sole caller runs `--quiet`, so the whole class produced zero output. MEASURED on the live corpus: 50 unresolvable targets and 75 missing items, previously invisible; the run emits 227 lines under `--quiet` where 403 ambiguity lines were the only output before.
      - Corpus-derived text is bounded by `_diag()` (120 chars, newlines escaped) and the ambiguity match list is truncated to 20 with a `+N more` suffix, because `_diag` bounds strings and a hit list is a list.
      - The per-instance listing is budgeted PER CLASS (100 each), never globally. A single shared budget was written first and measured: the 403 pre-existing ambiguity findings come first in corpus order and consumed all of it, listing 0 of 50 unresolvable and 15 of 75 missing -- a budget that silenced precisely the two classes this section added. Both now list in full.
      - The summary line stops being `--quiet`-gated whenever any class is non-zero. The suppression line points at "the summary line", and under `--quiet` that summary was not printed at all, so a truncated listing was the entire story for the only automatic caller.
- [x] Repair mode refuses a corpus whose `(directory, number)` slots collide, at rc 2 before any write
      `check_duplicate_resolver_key(nodes)` is called up front beside the existing duplicate-`id` refusal, and `_refuse` names the colliding pair.
      - The filed premise "cache_schema refuses such a cache at load, so this is defence in depth" was DISPROVEN while implementing: `build.py` builds the cache and `validate.py`'s own profile loads it happily (`check_duplicate_resolver_key`'s docstring says as much -- only `query.py` and MCP refuse outright). The real safety layer is §26's resolver, which fails closed on every spelling.
      - So the value here is DIAGNOSIS, measured on one tree: pre-§29 rc 0 with zero output and 4 stale stamps; with the refusal reverted rc 1 and four per-stamp `unresolvable` lines; as shipped rc 2 and one line naming the collision.
- [x] One repair-mode fixture per spelling, against the same collision, plus the classes and controls the review demanded
      Ten fixtures at the end of [`test_build.sh`](../../scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh) (`Section 29`): clean-corpus control (rc 0), unresolvable, missing-item, partial-repair-still-exits-1, the four-spelling collision, dry-run rc contract, and quiet-mode ambiguity naming item/target/stamp/hit-lines.
      - THREE mutation controls, all verified to fire: reverting the refusal (rc 2 -> four rc-1 reports), MOVING the refusal after `fix_line_numbers` (rewrites a stamp that must stay stale), and deleting the per-instance ambiguity line.
      - The collision fixture carries a fifth, independently repairable stamp. Without it every stamp was unrepairable anyway, so a late-refusing build produced an identical rc, message, hash and stale count -- the fixture could not see the ordering it documents (Codex test-coverage, [high]).
      - Budget fixtures assert EXACT counts (100 listed, 1 suppression line, exact totals under `--quiet`) and cover both rare classes under an ambiguity flood. A `-le` bound plus `grep -q` had left an off-by-one cap and a per-diagnostic suppression line -- which restores the O(n) growth -- both green (Codex re-adversarial, [medium]).
      - Two mutation fixtures FAILED CLOSED when these fixes moved the code they patch, which is the designed behavior; they were repaired to pin structure rather than message prose.
- [/] The auto-rewrite hook still swallows the two new failure classes -- operator-gated
      [`.claude/hooks/todo_graph_auto_rewrite.py:61`](../../.claude/hooks/todo_graph_auto_rewrite.py) emits its systemMessage only when `returncode == 1 AND "ambiguous" in combined`, then returns 0 regardless, so an unresolvable-or-missing-only run stays silent for the one caller that runs automatically. Raised by BOTH the design and adversarial reviews.
      - BLOCKER: `.claude/hooks/**` is control plane, which an unattended run may not edit; only an attended session can land it. Owned by §6, which created the hook, but §6 is stamped -- so it is parked here rather than appended there.
      - The fix is small and known: surface every non-zero result, with distinct messages for rc 1 (incomplete) and rc 2 (refused), update the exit-contract comment at line 61 and the `.claude/hooks/MANIFEST.md` row, and add an end-to-end fixture per class.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: repair mode fails visibly on targets it cannot resolve"`

**Test checkpoint:** a repair run over a corpus with one unresolvable target exits non-zero (or warns, per the recorded contract) instead of 0; a repair run over a resolver-key-colliding corpus refuses up front; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- 514/514 pass, of which 12 are this section's (`Section 29`, including three mutation controls).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: repair mode names every stamp it could not repair, counts a third class (`missing_items`), exits 1 on any of the three, and refuses at rc 2 on a resolver-key collision.
> - Runs through `main()`'s single call site (`fix_line_numbers` now returns a 5-tuple) and the existing `_refuse` path; no gate or CI job invokes repair mode.
> - Downstream: 125 previously-silent live failures (50 unresolvable, 75 missing) now emit diagnostics -- but only to a caller that reads them; the auto-rewrite hook still discards them.
> - Canonical doc: the exit contract sits in `validate.py`'s CLI header beside `--fix-line-numbers`, restated at the return site; rc 2 explicitly does NOT promise an untouched corpus.
> - Scope boundary: exit contract and collision refusal only; §32 and §33 were split out before implementation, and the hook fix is control plane.

> **Deferred:** [H] the auto-rewrite hook surfaces rc 1 only when the output contains "ambiguous", so it still discards the unresolvable and missing-item classes for the one caller that runs automatically (reason: `.claude/hooks/**` is control plane an unattended run may not edit) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §29 (item: "The auto-rewrite hook still swallows the two new failure classes -- operator-gated" at line 1523)

## 30. `--watch` Never Ticks on a History Change for the History-Consuming Verbs

> **Spawned-by:** §27 (split)

Split out of §27 on 2026-08-09, before either half was implemented. Both halves came from §24's reviews and both are about the history axis, but §27 asks what the identity FAILS TO SEE when it is computed, and this section asks why it never gets COMPUTED AGAIN. The machinery is disjoint: §27 works in `cache_schema.corpus_history_id` and `build.collect_git_timestamps`, this one in `query.watch_loop`.

`watch_loop` (`scripts/todo-graph/query.py:1800`) re-runs on `.md` events under `todo/`, via `inotifywait -m` when available and 2s mtime polling otherwise. An amend, a rebase, or a `git checkout` of a branch with identical corpus bytes moves the history while leaving every worktree byte identical, so no event fires and no tick happens. §24 routed `stale`, `stats` and `render --render-format gantt` through the stricter profile that checks `corpus_history_id`, but a watcher on one of those verbs sits on its last answer indefinitely -- the check it now declares never gets a chance to run (Codex adversarial, §24, [medium]).

This is a gap in a NEW capability, not a regression: watch mode never checked history before §24 either, and a tick that DOES run fails closed. That is why §24 filed it rather than hot-fixing it, and it is why the bar here is "the watcher eventually re-asks", not "the watcher is instantaneous".

- [x] Trigger is a 50.1us pathname snapshot of the git ref surface, polled every 2s; the 54.2ms identity runs only when it moves
      `_snapshot_history_trigger` (`query.py:2093`) stats `git rev-parse --git-path` HEAD / packed-refs / shallow and walks `refs/` RECURSIVELY. Measured 2026-08-09 on this repo: 50.1us median, 77.6us p95 over 12 entries -- 0.09% of the 54.2ms `corpus_history_id` it decides whether to ask, and 0.0025% of a core at one probe per poll, against 2.7% for polling the identity itself. The numbers are in the docstring.
      - A non-recursive stat of `refs/` was designed first and REJECTED by review as blind to the case this section exists for: a loose-ref write touches `refs/heads`, not `refs/`. Measured control on this repo -- `refs/` mtime 1786242163389184305 against `refs/heads` 1786275154647232807.
      - Pathnames, not inotify watches: git publishes `HEAD` and `packed-refs` by renaming a lockfile over them, which retires an inode watch silently, and watching their parent means recursing the git dir into `objects/`.
      - BOUNDED at 20,000 entries / 0.25s, because 12 entries measured here bounds nothing: a 100k-loose-ref repo would pay 100k stats every 2s on the thread that also drains inotify events. Over the ceiling the snapshot is marked INCOMPLETE -- never silently truncated -- and the axis says so once and degrades to a direct 60s identity probe.
- [x] Armed from `profile.requires_history`, so only `stale` / `stats` / `render --render-format gantt` pay anything
      `watch_loop(..., history=profile.requires_history)` (`query.py:3187`) reads the same profile the post-walk check reads, so trigger and check cannot disagree about which verbs consume a derived timestamp. A `ready` or `backlinks` watcher allocates no axis, polls nothing, and blocks indefinitely exactly as before.
- [x] A refused tick retries on a capped backoff (5/10/20/40/60s) instead of waiting for an unrelated edit
      `_watch_tick` now reports whether the refusal is worth retrying; only infrastructure refusals qualify (a bad request and a ceiling breach are deterministic and would just reprint). The OPENING run arms the retry too -- discarding its result was the first draft's bug, and it is the case most likely to hit a user, who starts the watcher precisely when something looks wrong.
      - An indeterminate probe gets its OWN sticky pending state and its own schedule (2/5/15/30/60s, never giving up), held apart from the tick budget: sharing one budget let ref churn during a git outage consume all five slots without a single tick, and once the churn stopped the watcher had neither a pending probe nor a deadline and displayed the old answer forever.
      - Path RESOLUTION is retried the same way, and "not a repository" is separated from "could not ask" -- collapsing both to None meant one transient git failure at start-up armed a permanently blind axis, reintroducing this section's own latch one layer up.
      - Capped on purpose: after the schedule a deterministic failure goes quiet and only a real event resumes it. Duplicate-envelope suppression was rejected -- the watch stream is one machine-readable envelope per tick, and hiding a repeat would under-report how many ticks ran.
- [x] Ten fixtures in `test_build.sh` (`Section 30`), two of them mutation controls, both branches proven
      Amend with byte-identical corpus ticks; unrelated commit does NOT; `ready` ignores the same amend; the snapshot sees a loose-ref write that `refs/` mtime alone misses; a refused tick emits >= 2 envelopes with the corpus provably untouched; an unresolvable git keeps the axis pending and re-publishes on recovery. Mutations: disarming the axis makes the amend invisible again, and dropping the retry signal latches at exactly one envelope.
      - WHICH BRANCH RAN IS ASSERTED, off a `watch: inotify|polling` marker on stderr. `inotifywait` is not installed on this host, so every watch fixture in the file had been running the polling loop -- including the one asserting inotify behaviour -- and 9r's `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` "forcing" named the directory inotify-tools installs into. Polling is now forced by `TODOGRAPH_WATCH_FORCE_POLL` and hard-fails; the inotify branch runs against a stub `inotifywait`. 9r repaired the same way.
      - Both mutants needed `PYTHONPATH` and a `build.py` symlink beside them -- without either they die on import or skip the rebuild, and a mutation fixture that expects the OLD behaviour reads that as a pass. 30d passed that way until the probe was checked.
- [x] Uncertainty never reads as "unchanged" -- the rule the review kept finding new routes around
      Five separate routes were closed: a truncated ref walk, a directory that could not be enumerated, a read that died mid-directory, a stat that failed for any reason other than genuine absence (ENOENT/ENOTDIR), and a symlinked ref entry whose target this walk must not follow. Each returns an INCOMPLETE snapshot, which degrades to direct identity polling -- slower and correct, where every one of them was previously fast and silently wrong.
- [x] The ceiling binds per ENTRY, counted once, and the deadline guards every blocking call
      A per-directory check is no bound when the fanout is inside one directory (ceiling 5 -> 302 stats). Double-counting directories to compensate then degraded `refs/pull/<n>/head` layouts permanently, so each object counts exactly once and the deadline is checked before every scandir, every `next(it)` and every post-`is_dir` stat.
- [x] Every retry path schedules exactly one future attempt, on its own budget
      Discovery and identity failures have separate backoff indices (a successful identity sample was resetting the discovery budget, so a persistent `--git-path` failure re-ran the 54.2ms walk every 2s forever); `pending` outranks `no_repo` when choosing the next deadline; and cadences are anchored to probe COMPLETION, so git slower than the cadence cannot return an already-expired cooldown.
- [x] Arming corrected to `requires_history AND check_stale` (review round 1)
      `load_and_validate` populates `info.history` only inside its `check_stale` branch, so on a non-canonical `--cache` the post-walk check is a no-op while an axis armed on the profile alone still probed the local repository. The section's own "they cannot disagree" claim was false as written; the fixtures moved onto the canonical cache path, which is the configuration that ships.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: --watch ticks when the history moves, not only the bytes"`

**Test checkpoint:** a watcher on `stale` re-runs after an amend that leaves the corpus byte-identical; a watcher on `ready` pays no history cost; a commit touching no `todo/` path fires no tick; the measured idle cost is recorded in the source; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- 535/535 pass, of which 21 are this section's (`Section 30`, including two mutation controls, both watch branches, and a control beside every negative assertion).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: a per-verb history axis in `watch_loop` -- a bounded 50.1us ref probe every 2s, the 54.2ms identity only when it moves, and three independent anti-latch schedules (refused tick, indeterminate probe, failed discovery).
> - Runs off `requires_history AND check_stale`, which is what §24's post-walk check can actually enforce; the profile alone would arm an axis for an inspection-mode reader that consumes no history.
> - Downstream: watches are armed and both axes baselined BEFORE the opening answer, closing a corpus-side gap too; `--watch` now names its branch on stderr, which is what makes either branch testable.
> - Canonical doc: the cost decision, the ceilings and the rejected alternatives sit in the `_snapshot_history_trigger` and `_HistoryAxis` docstrings, beside the code that pays them.
> - Scope boundary: the tick predicate is exactly `corpus_history_id`, so the equal-count shallow boundary stayed §31's; only the observable `shallow`-file half was closed here. (§31 SHIPPED 2026-08-09 and refuses a shallow corpus outright, so the predicate is never asked about a truncated history and there is no stale answer left for a watcher to display.)

> **Verified:** 2026-08-09 | commit `55f4bc9f0` + review fixes | 10/10 items | build OK | test_build 535/535 (21 this section), test-tooling 1319/1319, 28326 kernel + 17 user-mode, lint 0 errors
> **Accepted:** [M] CLOSED 2026-08-09 by §31: the tick predicate could not see an equal-count shallow boundary move, so a Gantt watcher kept displaying the pre-move answer; the refuse-vs-bind decision came down on REFUSING, so the predicate never reports on a truncated history at all (the observable `shallow`-file half was already closed here, ticking unconditionally) (RESOLVED 2026-08-13 in §31, commit `d5a695833`) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §31 (item: "Decide what a shallow corpus IS to this tool, and record the reasoning")
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-09 | Codex 12x (design, adversarial, adversarial, consistency, perf, re-adversarial x7) | 1H+18M+0L fixed, 1 accepted | scope: N/A (host-side Python tooling, no kernel surface); parity N/A (no user-visible OS surface)

-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §27`](#27-effective-history-identity-replace-refs-and-grafts) -- the section this was split from, which owns what the history identity is computed FROM (item: "Decide between rejecting replacement ancestry and folding it into the identity"); whatever probe it settles on is the probe this section triggers, so it ships first.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §22`](#22-querypy-fail-closed-through-every-transport) -- the section that owns `query.py`'s watch transport and its fail-closed contract (item: "Commit: `\"todo-graph: query.py fail-closed through CLI, MCP and watch transports\"`"); it is stamped, so this trigger is owned here rather than appended to it.
-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §31`](#31-an-equal-count-shallow-boundary-still-moves-created_at-under-an-unchanged-identity) -- the tick predicate is `corpus_history_id`, so it inherited that id's blind spot exactly (item: "Decide what a shallow corpus IS to this tool, and record the reasoning"). The observable half was closed here: a change to the `shallow` file ticks UNCONDITIONALLY without consulting the id. §31 SHIPPED 2026-08-09 and closed the rest by REFUSING a shallow corpus in both the reader probe and the producer walk, so the in-flight `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` override that no watcher could see is now a refusal rather than a silently stale answer -- this section needed no change for it.

## 31. An Equal-Count Shallow Boundary Still Moves `created_at` Under an Unchanged Identity

> **Spawned-by:** §27 (review)

Found by §27's adversarial review on 2026-08-09 and REPRODUCED before filing. §27 closed the replace-ref and graft axis by making both inert on every git call; this is the third mechanism on the same axis, and it is the one §27 could not close because closing it requires a decision about what the tool does in a shallow clone.

`corpus_history_id` projects the history onto `tip:count`. §24 believed the count caught every `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` case because the count is computed under the same effective git environment as the walk -- and it does catch the ones that CHANGE the count, which is why the shallow-DEEPENING case is genuinely covered. It does not catch an equal-count boundary move.

Reproduced 2026-08-09 on git 2.43.0, with a full-history control: C0 adds a TODO, C1 and C2 touch nothing under the corpus, C3 touches the TODO. The full history walks `{C0, C3}`; a boundary at C2 walks `{C2, C3}`, because git treats a shallow root's tree as an addition. Tip identical, count identical at 2, and `created_at` moves 1577836800 -> 1583020800. A cache built under one boundary is then certified against the other.

- [x] Decide what a shallow corpus IS to this tool, and record the reasoning
      DECIDED: a shallow corpus is REFUSED determinately. `corpus_history_id` brackets its tip/count walk with `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` and raises `REASON_STALE` naming `git fetch --unshallow`; `collect_git_timestamps` brackets its own walk the same way. Both refusals live beside the walks they guard rather than at a fixed line number.
      - BINDING WAS DESIGNED, REVIEWED AND REJECTED, and the reason is not the ABA objection this item predicted. The Codex design review found, and I reproduced before accepting, that `tip:count:oldest` STILL COLLIDES: merge an unrelated older leg whose root touches the corpus, move the boundary on the other leg, and the untouched leg keeps supplying the traversal's oldest commit -- tip, count AND oldest all hold at c8da803e/4/92240d84 while `created_at` moves 1577836800 -> 1640995200. Every fixed-endpoint projection has that shape of hole; only digesting the whole walk closes it, and §24 measured that at 312ms against 47.9ms.
      - AND BINDING WOULD CERTIFY A WRONG VALUE ANYWAY. `created_at` is documented as the file's first commit; under a boundary it is the timestamp of whatever commit the boundary landed on, because git compares a shallow root against the empty tree. Binding only makes an incorrect value consistently fresh, where refusing says the true answer is unavailable.
      - THE CI OBJECTION WAS SELF-INFLICTED AND IS REPAIRED HERE. `.github/workflows/todo-graph.yml:65` checks out `fetch-depth: 0`, then line 86 re-shallowed it with `git fetch --depth=1` -- verified 2026-08-09 on git 2.43.0 that one such fetch flips a full clone's `--is-shallow-repository` to true. Dropping `--depth` keeps it false with the base still reachable, so nothing in this repo pays for the refusal.
      - COST: 53.2ms median (min 49.5, max 60.6, n=15) against a 53.2ms sum-of-parts control across six subprocesses, the two probes ~2.2ms of it (~4%). A first reading of 185ms was a concurrent build in another session sharing this worktree, not the code; the sum-of-parts control is what separated them. Codex perf measured each producer probe at ~0.96ms against ~770ms of total build time and confirmed both are load-bearing.
- [x] Whatever ships, the `corpus_history_id` docstring stops naming §31 as the open owner
      `cache_schema.py` now states the axis is closed by removing the input rather than widening the projection, and records both rejected-binding arguments inline so the cheap fix is not re-attempted. `query.py`'s inherited-precision paragraph (§30's watch trigger) updated in the same pass.
- [x] Fixture for the chosen behavior, mutation-checked
      Six sub-tests in `scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`, 541/541 green. 31a asserts tip AND count EQUAL first, then the refusal, against an unshallow control. 31b pins the multi-root collision so binding cannot be reintroduced. 31c is the mutation: strip both bracketing calls and the boundary repo certifies byte-equal to the full one. 31d covers the producer walk, 31e the reader's post-walk probe, 31f the watch trigger.
      - The first run of 31a/31b FAILED, and the bug was the fixture's: a `-- todo/a.md` pathspec is resolved relative to `-C`, which is already the corpus root, so it matched nothing and both sides compared equal on empty strings. Non-empty assertions were added for exactly that -- the control had been passing vacuously.
      - 31d covers the PRODUCER, added after the adversarial review found the reader-side probe was not enough (below).
- [x] BOTH walks are bracketed, producer and reader, so they enforce one temporal policy
      Found by this section's adversarial review as [high] and REPRODUCED before accepting: `corpus_history_id` refuses when IT is asked, but on the normal path `collect_git_timestamps` mints the identity from its own walk, so the only later probe is the post-walk comparison. A walk that ran under a boundary since removed publishes `created_at` 2022-01-01 against the true 2020-01-01, and because this is the equal-count case the two ids compare EQUAL, so `moved` is False and nothing refuses.
      - `build.py` now probes `--is-shallow-repository` immediately before AND after its walk and refuses on either reading non-`false`, binding the answer to that walk generation. `build_cache` catches the refusal and exits 3 rather than falling through to the provenance-rescue path.
      - The READER needed the same bracket, and a later consistency round caught that it had not got one ([high]): `corpus_history_id` probed only BEFORE its tip/count reads, so a corpus becoming shallow after that probe and staying shallow across them is a SINGLE transition -- and in the equal-count case it returns the full history's identity, certifying instead of refusing. Both walks now bracket, which is the only reason the two sides describe one policy rather than two.
      - HONEST RESIDUE, stated rather than claimed closed: this closes every SINGLE transition in either direction. A boundary installed AND removed wholly inside one walk still reads `false` twice. Closing that needs the walk to report its own boundary, which git does not expose. The re-adversarial round confirmed the residue is the only one and that it extends to the gaps immediately either side of the log subprocess, since the probes bracket the walk rather than binding it atomically.
      - AND ONE ACCEPTED FIXTURE LIMIT, recorded rather than left silent: 31d holds shallowness STABLE, so it fails only when BOTH probes are removed -- either one alone still satisfies it. Sensitivity to a single probe needs a deterministic mid-walk transition, which the fixture cannot stage; Codex classed this non-material.
- [x] The watch trigger follows the EFFECTIVE boundary file, not the default one
      Found by this section's final adversarial round as [medium] and verified at `query.py:2196`. `rev-parse --git-path shallow` resolves the DEFAULT file even under `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` -- the same asymmetry §24 cited when it rejected a file digest -- while the new refusal honours the override. A watcher started under an override naming an ABSENT file that is later populated sees no ref move and no watched file move, so `_HistoryAxis.poll` never re-asks and `stale` / `stats` / Gantt watch mode keep serving an answer a direct query now refuses.
      - Fixed HERE rather than filed: §30 owns the trigger but is stamped and closed, and it was §31's env-sensitive refusal that created the mismatch. The override path joins the trigger as the effective boundary and the default stays watched beside it, for one extra `stat` per poll. Fixture 31f, with a control asserting the no-override shape is unchanged.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: close the shallow-boundary axis in the history identity"`

**Test checkpoint:** a corpus walked under two different equal-count shallow boundaries is either refused or produces two different identities, and the fixture proving it fails when the mechanism is reverted; a non-shallow repository pays no measurable extra cost; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Notes:**
> - Decision: REFUSE, not bind. The two options were not equally available -- binding was reproduced COLLIDING on a multi-root history, so the choice was between refusing and shipping a projection with a known hole.
> - Both walks bracket. The reader and the producer each probe `--is-shallow-repository` before and after their own walk, so they enforce one temporal policy rather than two. The reader's second probe was missing until a consistency round caught it.
> - Stated residue: a boundary installed AND removed wholly inside one walk reads `false` twice. Closing it needs the walk to report its own boundary, which git does not expose.
> - Cost: six subprocesses, 53.2ms median against a 53.2ms sum-of-parts control; the two probes are ~2.2ms (~4%), and `rev-list --count` is 47.0ms of the rest.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- 541/541 pass, of which 6 are this section's (`Section 31`: the reproduced equal-count boundary, the multi-root collision that killed the binding design, a mutation control, one refusal fixture per bracketed walk, and the watch-trigger boundary check).
