## 39. The Shared Fence Tracker Becomes Container-Aware

> **Spawned-by:** §36 (split)

Split out of §36 at authoring time. This is the only item in the original filing that is NEW DESIGN rather than adoption, and it changes the fence rule itself for every consumer §36 and §38 route through it -- so it lands after both, when there is exactly one place to change.

- [x] Make `fence_step` apply CommonMark's 0-3-space rule after the list-container prefix
      `fence_step` applies the rule to the PHYSICAL line, but CommonMark applies it after stripping the enclosing list-container prefix -- so a fence indented five spaces under `100. docs` is a valid block this tracker does not open, and a checklist example inside it is indexed as a real item (Codex adversarial, §35 round 2, reproduced by probe).
      - The naive repair is CommonMark-WRONG and must not be taken: "treat any indent as a fence" breaks a 4-space marker at root, which really is an indented code block. §35's `overindented_fence` fixture pins that and must stay green.
      - The honest fix tracks list-container indentation, which is a Markdown block parser rather than a regex. That is the whole reason this is its own section.
      - SHIPPED as a container PHASE in `cache_schema.py`: `_match_containers` / `_open_containers` consume the enclosing blockquote and list-item prefixes, then hand the remainder to the unchanged `fence_step`, so the leaf rules keep one definition. Blockquotes are containers too and were not in the original filing: of the 105 fence lines the tracker was missing, 27 were blockquote-prefixed and 77 list-indented.
      - A blank line does NOT close a list container (Codex design review, [high]): `100. docs` / blank / five-space fence is one item containing a fenced block, and closing at the blank would have leaked its `- [ ]` content. A blockquote is the opposite. Both verified against markdown-it-py before implementing.
      - `todo-reflow.py`'s indent-stripping fallback was retired in the same change, as its own comment instructed -- a heuristic superset cannot tell a container fence from root indented code, and leaving it would keep that tool answering a different question from every other consumer.
- [x] Route `validate.py:_scan_markdown` through the shared full-document scan, terminal flags included
      Filed from §38's consistency review. `_scan_markdown` (`scripts/todo-graph/validate.py:1919`) shares only `fence_step` and re-implements comment state, masking order and EOF behaviour independently, so unlike `todo_fence.scan_text` and `build.py:1030` it cannot return terminal `unclosed_fence` / `unclosed_comment` flags -- it silently treats the remainder as hidden where the other two REFUSE. That is already a contract disagreement about the same document, and it will drift again the next time `fence_scan` learns a block construct.
      - It belongs HERE rather than in §38: §38 is gate-parser ADOPTION, and making the validator refuse an unclosed construct changes validator verdicts corpus-wide, which is a design change of the kind this section owns.
      - Acceptance oracle is a differential like every other adopter's: `validate.py` verdicts unchanged over the corpus except where a document is genuinely unreadable, and a fixture proving producer and validator now agree about an unclosed fence.
      - SHIPPED: `_scan_markdown(lines, mask)` now takes the shared scan, the caller runs it ONCE for both streaming passes, and an unclosed fence or comment goes to `_refuse` (rc 2) -- the INFRASTRUCTURE channel, not a graph Finding. That distinction is the point: rc 1 means "validation reached a verdict about the corpus", so a Finding would tell automation that an UNREADABLE document is an actionable corpus defect. `unclosed_reason()` MOVED to `cache_schema` so the validator quotes the same sentence as the four gates without a `sys.path` insertion.
      - Differential: validator output byte-identical over the corpus (0 of 281 files trip either flag), so this changed which way the check fails, not any live verdict.
- [x] Decide whether Check 7 and Check 17 honour the commit-gate exclusion set, then make the banner true either way
      Filed from §38's consistency review, which found the scope banner over-claiming: `gate_excluded()` (`scripts/lint.sh:82`) has ZERO callers, Check 7 uses `GATE_EXCLUDED_TODO` only to downgrade a stale-cache error (`lint.sh:609`) while still reporting every finding in a current cache, and Check 17 does not consult it at all. §38 corrected the banner to name only Checks 10/11/24; the underlying decision is untouched.
      - The decision is not cosmetic: making Check 7 exclude per file CHANGES which commits it blocks, so it needs the same corpus differential every other gate change in this TODO has had.
      - Whichever way it goes, wire the surviving consumers through `gate_excluded()` so the rule is stated once, and update the `.githooks/pre-commit` comment, which still names Checks 7/17/24.
      - DECIDED: Check 7 now honours the exclusion set for its FINDINGS. Its finding is exactly the shape the set exists for -- an unattended run stamps an item `[x]` while the implementation is still in flight, and an attended commit touching only `scripts/` is blocked by a TODO it never opened (three times on 2026-08-07).
      - The premise was half WRONG and the code is why. Check 17 has honoured the set all along at `lint.sh:1416`; the banner's claim that it "does not consult it at all" was false when written. So the banner had been wrong in BOTH directions, and now names Checks 7/10/11/17/24.
      - Wiring is a `gate_exclude_lines` stdin filter rather than the per-path `gate_excluded()`, because Check 7 reports at the SOURCE file that defines the stub and names its TODO only inside the message -- a filter anchored on the leading path field would have matched no todo path ever and been a silent no-op.
      - The `.githooks/pre-commit` comment is left as-is: it names Checks 7/17/24, which this change makes TRUE, and `.githooks/**` is control plane the unattended run may not edit. Naming 10/11 there too is cosmetic and operator-gated.
- [/] Reconcile the OTHER CommonMark divergences the §36 review surfaced -- two shipped, HTML blocks split to §42
      Filed rather than fixed in §36 on measurement: each is the same producer-versus-CommonMark class this section owns, each has **ZERO live corpus instances**, and none is a regression -- every one predates §36. Chasing them inside §36 is exactly the unbounded scope the §36/§38/§39 split exists to prevent.
      - **EOF-consuming HTML blocks other than comments.** CommonMark treats an unclosed `<script>` / `<pre>` / `<style>` / `<textarea>`, a processing instruction (`<?`), a declaration (`<!A...`) and CDATA as an HTML block through its terminator or EOF; `fence_scan` enters HTML state only for `<!--`. Probe: `<script>` followed by `## 9. Hidden` yields one CommonMark `html_block` while the producer reports the heading. Measured 2026-08-10: **0** such openers outside a fence corpus-wide.
      - **Indented ATX headings.** CommonMark allows 1-3 leading spaces before `##`, and `validate.py` accepts them, but `_walk_section_headings` and `_walk_stamped_items` both anchor at column 0 -- so an indented `## 1.` is invisible to the producer and visible to the validator, which is the erasure direction. Measured 2026-08-10: **0** indented `## N.` headings corpus-wide. Fix with ONE shared matcher used by both walks, not two.
      - **Lone-CR line endings.** `validate.py:_scan_markdown` splits on `\n` only, while `build.py:scan_body` uses `splitlines()`, which also breaks on a lone CR (and on `\x0b`, `\x0c`, `U+2028`). A CR-delimited body is therefore two different documents to the two halves. Measured 2026-08-10: **0** files under `todo/` contain any CR byte. Fix by normalising once, before both scans.
      - SHIPPED, two of three. Lone CR: `cache_schema.normalize_newlines()` folds every break `splitlines()` honours, and both entry points call it, so the two halves cannot disagree by construction. Indented ATX: one shared `SECTION_HEADING_RE` (0-3 spaces, matching the validator) now serves both `build.py` walks, which had TWO regexes differing in indent AND in whether a title-less `## 5.` opens a section.
      - SPLIT OUT to §42: the HTML-block divergence. Adding EOF-consuming types 1-5 without extending the terminal contract would mask to EOF while both flags read clean -- the exact silent erasure `fence_scan` was hardened against (Codex design review, [high]). Type-6 `<details>` is live (30 lines, 2 files) but blank-line-terminated and carries no structural token; excluding it strains the single boolean mask (Codex, [medium]). Both need the contract to carry block KIND, so they are one section, not a line.
- [x] Prove a NIL differential over the already-repaired corpus
      This section's input is the corpus AFTER §36's repairs, so its own acceptance oracle is nil: the container-aware parser must change no node and no item-index entry. Anything that moves is the parser being wrong, not it finding something -- the things it would have found were already fixed.
      - HISTORICAL, and NOT this section's oracle: the "ZERO live instances" premise this section was filed under was FALSE, disproven by §36's round-3 review. Measured 2026-08-10 against a real CommonMark parser, TWO files opened a fence inside a checklist item and then exited that container with an unindented table, so CommonMark ended the fence early and read the apparent closer as a new root opener -- swallowing four `## N.` headings and two closing-matter headings.
      - §36 repaired both by indenting the container-exiting table (`todo/15-installer-release/TODO-04-release-qa.md`, `todo/15-installer-release/TODO-05-github-release-community.md`), which is why the differential here is nil rather than two files. The repo's physical-line tracker could see neither, which is exactly why the original census read clean.
      - Re-run the CommonMark scan as a REGRESSION check, not as this section's differential: zero swallowed structure across all corpus files, both before and after.
      - MEASURED 2026-08-11 with markdown-it-py over all 281 files, probe carrying two controls that both fired as predicted: divergent files 8 -> 2, and the 2 remaining are exactly §42's `<details>` blocks. Every one of the 105 fence lines now agrees with CommonMark.
      - ONE DIFFERENTIAL IS NOT NIL, AND THAT ONE IS A REPAIR. `checklist_item_leads` moved on exactly one corpus file -- this one -- recovering THREE real `- [x]` items it had been hiding. The mechanism is the round-3 finding demonstrated on live data: item 2000 documents the syntax as `` `<!--` ``, HEAD searched the RAW lead for `<!--`, latched on the backticked example, truncated that item's name at the backtick and swallowed every item after it. `--fix-line-numbers` was reading a corpus with three items missing.
      - The nil differential HOLDS where it was claimed and NOT where it was assumed: the emitted cache is byte-identical (control -- a HEAD rebuild reproduced the saved baseline first), and so are validator output and `todo-reflow --diff` output over the corpus. But the MASK moves on 135 lines; it is the INDEX that is nil, because none of those lines carried a heading, item, row or stamp.
      - The corpus was NOT a sufficient oracle, and twelve review rounds proved it. Acceptance is now a STATEFUL differential against markdown-it-py: exhaustive 4-line documents over a 36-state alphabet (1,679,616), a 40-state run carrying multi-line link definitions (2,560,000), a 47-state union at 3-line depth (103,823), and a 5-line 16-state run (1,048,576). Independent prefix/marker combinations -- the shape tried first -- cannot generate cross-line block-state transitions, and that is where nearly every defect lived.
      - EACH ALPHABET EXTENSION FOUND NEW DEFECTS, which is the honest read of the method: "exhaustive" is only ever exhaustive over the states it can spell. The claim "one residual class" was made after the 2.56M run and was FALSE -- the next round found two more classes the alphabet could not express (nested tab stops, and sibling items in the 1-5 space band).
      - Residual divergence is TWO named classes, both filed in §42 with acceptance tests: multi-line link reference definitions (66 of 2,560,000 in the alphabet that can spell them) and a lazy-continuation/nested-container interaction (4 of 1,679,616; 2 of 103,823). Both are 0 instances corpus-wide, and the second is the worse kind -- it makes the producer refuse a valid document -- which is why it is owned rather than merely noted.
      - Cost: `fence_scan` 23.5ms -> ~160ms over the corpus and the builder 1.02s -> ~1.07s against its 2s budget. `mask_code_spans` needed THREE cuts, each failing on input a repository controls: rescanning the suffix per unmatched run was quadratic (5.6s on a 336 KB line), indexing every run position fixed the time and not the space (95 bytes of Python objects per input byte, so a permitted 16 MiB line was ~1.5 GiB), and the shipped version is a single forward pass keeping only the earliest live opener per run length -- 14ms and 9 bytes/byte, with the output list allocated only when something is masked. The first regression was NOT the container work at all -- `str.translate` in the new normaliser cost 855ms over 11.1 MB and tripped that budget; a compiled pre-search made it free.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: the shared fence tracker becomes container-aware"`

**Test checkpoint:** a fence indented five spaces under an ordered-list marker opens a real block and its `- [ ]` content is not indexed; a 4-space fence marker at root is still an indented code block (§35's `overindented_fence` fixture green); the corpus index is byte-identical across the change; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` (233 assertions; `test_container_aware_fences` pins every finding from the twelve review rounds) and `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (586/586). No kernel test surface -- this is host tooling.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped a CommonMark container phase in `cache_schema.fence_scan`: blockquote and list-item prefixes are consumed before the leaf 0-3-space rule, and a leaf block now dies with its container.
> - `todo-reflow.py`'s indent-stripping fallback and its ambiguity refusal were retired in the same change, so every consumer answers one fence question; `validate.py` now takes the shared scan and refuses an unclosed construct with rc 2, as the producer already did.
> - Check 7's FINDINGS joined the commit-gate exclusion set through `gate_exclude_lines`; its rc-7 population gate stays repo-wide and the banner now says exactly that, having been wrong in both directions before.
> - Differentials are nil where claimed: cache, validator output and reflow rewrite output all byte-identical over 281 files; the MASK moves on 135 lines that carry no structure. `fence_scan` 23.5ms -> ~168ms, builder 1.02s -> ~1.07s against a 2s budget.
> - Acceptance rests on stateful CommonMark differentials (~5.5M generated documents across five alphabets), not on the corpus: 39 findings over twelve adversarial rounds, every one reproduced before it was fixed and pinned as a fixture.
> - Two residual classes remain, 0 corpus instances, filed in §42 with acceptance tests. THREE attempted fixes for the second were reverted on measurement (4 -> 704, 4 -> 5023, and a terminal-flag mitigation that suppressed genuine unclosed fences); the flag is reported honestly and the trade is taken fail-CLOSED on purpose.
> - Canonical doc: the container rules live at `cache_schema.py` beside `_match_containers`; the retirement rationale lives at `todo-reflow.py`.
> - Scope boundary: HTML blocks other than `<!--` comments are NOT in this section -- they need the terminal contract to carry the block kind, which is §42.

## 40. An Inputs Section Marker Keeps Its Closing Backtick, So One Dependency Is Two Edges

> **Spawned-by:** §37 (review)
> **User impact:** The TODO dependency graph under-reports Inputs edges. `query.py:1094` keys edge identity on `(target_path, target_section)`, so `§7` and a backticked `§7` are two different edges naming the same dependency -- and `backlinks` / `deferred` / `blocked` are what the runner and a contributor read to decide what is ready to build. Work that looks unblocked can sit behind an edge nobody queries.

> **Continuation waiver** (review-spawn at depth 3, the limit):
> - **user_impact:** as above -- 508 of the 945 live Inputs rows carry the backticked spelling, so this is the majority case rather than an edge case.
> - **not_parkable:** a parked item in §37 would be stranded, because §37 is stamped DONE in the same pass and the fixpoint loop never revisits a DONE section. §38 and §39 are open but own the fence tracker, an unrelated subject.
> - **severity_trend:** falling and converging -- §37's review went 1H+4M, then 1M, then this 1M, and this is the FIRST finding that is not a defect §37 introduced.
> - **surface:** `build.py:extract_inputs_xrefs` (the bullet surface's `target_section` capture) and its consumers in `query.py` -- the same producer family §37 consolidated, one field over.

Found by §37's re-adversarial round 2, and PRE-EXISTING rather than a regression: §37's corpus differential is byte-identical, and the baseline it was measured against already contained all 508 rows. The bullet surface captures the section marker with a non-space run, which swallows the closing backtick when a code span wraps the whole path-and-section construct; the table surface removes the span delimiters before parsing and emits a clean marker. So the two Inputs surfaces describe the same reference differently -- the split-brain §37 removed for the TARGET field and did not reach for the SECTION field.

- [x] Normalise the section marker on BOTH XREF subtrees, in the shared parser, and prove it with a per-clause differential
      Shipped as one rule in `cache_schema.py:_XREF_SECTION_RE`: the marker capture stops at the code-span delimiter, and the delimiter run is CONSUMED rather than merely excluded, so all four producer surfaces agree.
      - The differential ran PER CLAUSE over all 3,229 live clauses: 1,062 section values change and nothing else moves -- 0 targets, 0 item names, 0 malformed verdicts, and an identical clause count, so the change cannot have fabricated an edge. At cache level exactly 640 rows move (508 Inputs, 132 stamp), across 80 distinct values of which 66 lose a bare delimiter and 14 lose a delimiter plus the prose punctuation outside it, and `target_section` is the only field that differs on any of them.
      - The control fires. Sub-tests 40a/40b re-install the old pattern and require the fixture to fail under it, and 40d requires the LIVE corpus to still contain the delimiter shape, so a corpus that stopped containing spans cannot pass the invariant vacuously. Patching the controls was itself load-bearing: once the parser gained a second rule they kept passing while no longer reaching the code under test, and each now restores BOTH names.
      - The delimiter rule is SCOPED to a span that is genuinely open, and BOTH halves of that -- whether a span is open, and where it ends -- are ASKED OF the segmenter rather than computed here. FOUR successive cheaper answers were each wrong in the same direction, every one fabricating an edge or misattaching an item: consuming the delimiter context-free, reading the target token's backtick parity (defeated by an ESCAPED backtick in a link label, which is not an opener but still counts), matching a trailing backtick RUN (which stops at a shorter literal run inside a doubled-delimiter span), and walking forward while a position looked masked (which cannot tell a space after the span from one inside it, so an ADJACENT span was swallowed). Codex adversarial rounds 1-4.
      - The segmenter had already paid for every case that bit: run-aware, escaping applied to openers only, one linear pass in bounded memory (§39). It knows each span's end exactly at the moment it closes one, so `_mask_marking_span_ends` hands that over via a sentinel on the span's final character; `mask_code_spans` keeps its space-fill contract untouched and every existing caller is unaffected. The mask is computed ONCE PER LINE in `iter_xref_clauses`, so a many-clause stamp stays linear -- sub-test 37h is the pin, and per-clause recomputation is the quadratic shape it catches. Outside a span there is no delimiter to stop at, so the old non-space run stands there unchanged.
      - 86 clause ends move, and both directions are accounted for: EARLIER where the old non-space run had absorbed trailing prose (a paren or a whole link tail) into the marker, and LATER where the clause now runs to the closing delimiter across a marker written with an internal space such as a comma-joined list. The captured VALUE is unchanged in every one, and the clause COUNT is identical, so nothing was suppressed and nothing fabricated.
      - IT REACHED THE STAMP SUBTREE, which the item as authored did not anticipate: one parser serves both, 132 stamp rows carried the identical defect, and repairing only the Inputs surfaces would have re-created the split-brain one subtree over instead of removing it.
      - The delimiter is CONSUMED because excluding it is not enough: the item parenthetical is matched at the position the section match ended, so a capture that merely stops before the backtick strands that position and drops the item name -- 160 live clauses, caught by the pre-edit design review [medium] before any of this shipped.
- [x] Establish what the 508 rows currently cost before changing them
      Measured by diffing every query answer across a v4 and a v5 cache, and the honest answer is materially SMALLER than this section's own User-impact line predicted.
      - Labels only: `backlinks` changes 123 of 232 answers (586 rows), `deferred` 9 answers (80 rows), `deferred-by` 30 answers (80 rows). After normalising the spelling the row MULTISETS are identical, so the handful of rows that looked like content changes were sort reordering -- `section` is a sort key on all three.
      - Scheduling is untouched: `blocked`, `ready`, `section-ready`, `section-blocked` (1,169 rows), `section-blocking` (248 rows), `stats` and `orphans` are byte-identical across the two caches.
      - THE USER-IMPACT CLAIM ABOVE IS PARTLY DISPROVEN, and is left standing rather than quietly rewritten because a waiver that edits its own prediction after the measurement is not a waiver. `query.py:1094` keys edge identity on the `sections[].depends_on` INTEGER subtree, not on `inputs_xrefs`, so no readiness verdict ever split on this. What the 640 rows actually cost is a label naming a section under a spelling no section has, on the three answers a reader consults -- worth fixing, and not the scheduling defect that was predicted.
- [x] Decide whether `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` moves again
      Yes: 4 -> 5, and this is the FIRST spend of that integer whose live differential is not nil.
      - The declared rule at `cache_schema.py:137` reserves it for a field that keeps its name and type and changes its meaning, which is exactly this. v2 -> v3 and v3 -> v4 were both spent on corpora that were byte-identical across the change; here 640 rows disagree outright between a v4 and a v5 producer, so the case is strictly stronger than the two that already justified a bump.
      - And the bump now has a regression of its own (sub-test 40g). Sub-test 25c is version-AGNOSTIC by construction -- it writes `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION - 1`, which proves the refusal MECHANISM and stays green if the constant is reverted, since it would then simply write 3. 40g pins the LITERAL historical 4, so reverting the bump makes that artifact current, the refusal stops firing, and a test fails (Codex test-coverage, [medium]).
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: an Inputs section marker means one edge, not two"`

**Test checkpoint:** the bullet and table forms of a whole-span reference both emit the same target and the same clean section marker; the differential moves ONLY `target_section`, on exactly the rows whose marker carried a code-span delimiter; a control with the normalisation disabled reproduces the split; a whole-span stamp still yields its item name; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (596/596; sub-tests 40a surface parity, 40b delimiter-consumed + item name, 40c passthrough, 40d live-corpus invariant, 40e opening-backtick, 40f table punctuation parity, 40g literal-v4 refusal, 40h escaped/doubled/nested delimiters, 40i punctuation keeps the item, 40j bounded scan -- each with its own control).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: two context-selected rules in the shared parser -- the marker stops at a code-span delimiter and consumes it where a span is open AT the marker, and keeps the old non-space run where none is.
> - Integrates via `parse_xref_clause`, which asks `mask_code_spans` which rule applies (one mask per line); the table branch adds one caller-side strip of a prose character off a closed span.
> - Downstream: 640 cache rows change value (508 Inputs, 132 stamp) and `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` moves 4 -> 5; `backlinks` / `deferred` / `deferred-by` labels correct, every readiness answer byte-identical.
> - Oracle: per-clause differential over all 3,229 live clauses, replayed against the real HEAD producer -- 1,062 markers change, 0 targets, 0 item names, 0 malformed verdicts, identical clause count; 0 markers left carrying a delimiter.
> - Scope boundary: a marker's INTERNAL vocabulary is not re-grammared (a digits-with-joins grammar was rejected for truncating unicode-dash ranges); the trailing-comma spelling and the table surface's non-punctuation tail are left, the latter parked with its blocker named.

## 41. The Two Mutating Repair Tools Adopt the Shared Fence Tracker

> **Spawned-by:** §38 (split)

Split out of §38 on 2026-08-11, before code, on the sequencer's SPLIT-RECOMMENDED verdict. §38 kept the four parsers that return blocking VERDICTS; this section takes the two that REWRITE TODO files in place, because the two halves are proven by different oracles and share no file. A gate adopter is right when its verdict is unchanged over the corpus; a mutating adopter is right when the corpus is byte-identical after it runs. Worst first was the original ordering argument, and it still holds -- a fence either of these misreads is a file EDIT, so both run under `--diff` over every file before `--write` is ever considered. Adoption only: `fence_mask()` shipped in §36. (The oracle is the CLI's OWN target set, which is 281 files -- this section first wrote 232, the graph-builder's population, leaving 49 eligible mutation targets unproven.)

- [x] Route the two MUTATING repair tools through the mask
      `reflow()` takes one verbatim mask instead of an `incode` bool, and `todo-section-order.py` threads ONE scan through both of its heading walks.
      - `scripts/todo-reflow.py`: `verbatim_scan`/`verbatim_mask` union the shared mask with `_loose_step`, which runs the shared `fence_step` over the indent-stripped line to cover the container-indented fences the tracker's 0-3-space bound cannot see (§39's known limit). The fallback steps only on lines the mask did not claim, so an inner run inside a fenced block cannot open it.
      - `scripts/todo_fence.py` re-exports `fence_step` for that use. A first cut of the fallback was a bare toggle and closed a five-space-indented ```` block on the ``` inside it, reflowing the body -- delegating to the primitive is what fixed it.
      - `scripts/todo-section-order.py`: `scan()` is the single per-document scan, passed to `parse`, `sections_after_closing`, `is_ordered`, `reorder` and `_check_placement`, so a fenced `## N.` example can no longer be read as a real section or split a block during a repair.
- [x] Fix the SECOND heading scan in `todo-section-order.py`, not just `parse()`
      Both walks now take the same mask via the optional `sc=` parameter, so `reorder` and `_check_placement` (lint Check 22b's blocking gate) cannot disagree about what a heading is.
      - A masked-to-EOF document is REFUSED rather than read as empty: `parse()` returns `None`, `main()` exits 2 without touching the file, and `_check_placement()` prints the reason on stdout where Check 22b counts it as an error. Unreadable and non-UTF-8 targets take the same refusal instead of being skipped, which had let `--check` return 0 for files it never scanned.
      - Containment came with it: `_targets()` drops a symlinked `todo/` root and symlinked `*.md` entries, so the two checks this tool backs cannot traverse whatever a replaced root points at.
- [x] Prove both tools are corpus-neutral before landing
      Per tool: `--diff` captured before and after over the CLI's real target set -- **281 files, not the 232 this section first wrote**, since `_targets()` globs all of them -- byte-identical for both, plus `--check-placement` clean and `--check` rc 0 corpus-wide.
      - `scripts/lint.sh` Check 19 was the missing half: it discarded the tool's exit code, so the new refusals reached it as silence. It now validates the exit-code/output envelope, guards corpus discovery three ways, and reports a refusal as an ERROR.
      - Measured across the 281 files: 5 rely on the container-fence fallback and 0 change the reflow verdict, which is what makes the refusal rule free today.
- [x] Make both rewrites atomic and race-checked (post-commit review, [high])
      `todo_fence.replace_atomically()` writes a sibling temp file, fsyncs it, `os.replace`s it and fsyncs the directory, after re-reading the file to confirm it still holds the bytes the rewrite was computed from. Both tools used to `open(path,"w")` / `write_text`, truncating BEFORE the new content was committed -- a full disk or an interruption left a TODO empty, and an edit landing between read and write was silently overwritten. `todo-section-order.py` also let that write error escape as rc 1, its "out of order" code.
- [x] Contain the corpus, bound the section number, and stop reflowing twice (post-commit review)
      Three separate fail-open paths the whole-repo pass found that the eight scoped rounds did not.
      - `_targets()` walks with `os.walk(followlinks=False)` and prunes symlinked directories: filtering only the final candidate missed `todo/link -> /outside`, so Checks 22/22b could traverse an external tree and `--fix` could rewrite a file outside the repository.
      - `SECTION_RE` is bounded to 9 digits and `overlong_section()` names anything longer: `int()` refuses a conversion over 4,300 digits, so a 5,000-digit heading exited 1 with empty stdout and lint read the crash as clean. Checks 22 and 22b now validate the exit-code/output envelope, as Check 19 already did.
      - The ambiguity proof compares masks before reflowing a second time: equal masks give equal output, and 276 of 281 files have equal masks, so the second pass was ~19% of an every-commit command.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: the two mutating repair tools adopt the shared fence tracker"`

**Test checkpoint:** a reflow and a section-order repair over a tilde fence, a 4-backtick block containing a shorter run, and an unterminated fence all leave the file byte-identical; `--check-placement` is clean on a fixture whose fenced block contains a recognised closing heading followed by a real numbered section; every fixture fails when its fence-blind walk is restored; the corpus is byte-identical under both tools' `--diff`; `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` (122 assertions, wired into `scripts/test-tooling.sh`); plus `python3 scripts/todo-reflow.py --selftest` and `python3 scripts/todo-section-order.py --selftest`, the latter newly wired.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: both mutating repair tools take the shared tracker -- `verbatim_scan` in `todo-reflow.py`, and one `scan()` threaded through both heading walks in `todo-section-order.py`.
> - Integrates via `scripts/todo_fence.py`, which now re-exports `fence_step` so the container-fence fallback delegates the closer character/length rule rather than defining a second matcher.
> - Downstream: lint Check 19 became an ERROR path for a refused document and gained corpus-discovery guards; `todo-section-order.py --selftest` and a clean-corpus gate are now wired into `test-tooling.sh`.
> - Oracle: both tools `--diff` byte-identical over all 281 `todo/**/*.md` files, before vs after; 5 rely on the fallback and 0 change verdict.
> - Scope boundary: the fallback is a heuristic that retires when §39 makes the tracker container-aware; no shared corpus-list refactor across Checks 19/22/22b.

> **Verified:** 2026-08-11 | commit `25eaa820d` | 6/6 items | build OK | lint 0 errors | test-tooling 1323/1323 | test_todo_fence 130 assertions | both tools byte-identical over 281 files
> **Deferred:** [M] a fifth TODO parser, `.claude/hooks/todo_wrap_reminder.py`, still carries the naive `incode` toggle (reason: control plane, the unattended run may not edit it) -> XREF: 00-infrastructure/TODO-06 §38 (item: "`.claude/hooks/todo_wrap_reminder.py:109` (`_blocks`) and `:177` (`long_continuation`) each carry their own `incode` toggle -- PARKED, control plane, operator-gated" at line 2039)
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-11 | Codex 13x (design, adversarial x9, consistency x2, perf) over 9 rounds | 3H+9M fixed, 1 deferred control-plane with reciprocal owner | scope: N/A (host tooling -- no kernel/boot surface, and no domain code-quality skill covers `scripts/`); parity N/A (no user-visible OS surface)

## 42. HTML Blocks Join the Tracker, and the Terminal Contract Carries the Block Kind

> **Spawned-by:** §39 (review)
> **User impact:** Two shapes reach the graph wrong. A `## N.` heading or `- [x]` item written inside a `<details>` block is indexed as real structure while CommonMark hides it, so the runner can act on a section that does not render; and an unclosed `<script>`, processing instruction, declaration or CDATA block masks every later section to EOF while BOTH terminal flags read clean, which is the silent erasure `fence_scan` was hardened against for fences and comments.

> **Continuation waiver** (review-spawn at depth 3, the limit):
> - **user_impact:** as above. The EOF-consuming class has 0 live openers; the type-6 class is live at 30 lines across 2 files, and carries no structural token TODAY -- which is a corpus observation, not an invariant, and is exactly the reviewer's point.
> - **not_parkable:** a park in §39 would be stranded -- §39 is stamped in this same pass and the oracle never revisits a DONE section -- and `stranded_deferrals.py` re-opens only parks naming a CROSS-TODO owner, which an intra-TODO park cannot have.
> - **severity_trend:** §39's design round was 2H+2M; the two carried here are the two whose fix requires a CONTRACT change rather than a defect in what shipped, so this is a scope split rather than an unresolved finding.
> - **surface:** `cache_schema.fence_scan`'s terminal contract and its nine consumers (`build.py`, `validate.py`, `todo_fence.py`, the four gates, `todo-reflow.py`, `format-md-tables.py`).

Filed by §39's design review rather than shipped inside it, on the reviewer's own argument: adding EOF-consuming HTML states to a scan whose terminal result is two booleans produces a mask that erases the document with nothing to report, which is strictly worse than the gap it closes. The section number is 42 because 41 was taken by the repair-tool adoption; the file is over the 40-section soft cap, and this is deliberately filed HERE anyway because TODO-06 is the domain-correct owner of the fence tracker and a `-part-2` file is not an acceptable destination.

- [x] Extend the terminal contract to carry the block KIND -- `fence_scan` returns a non-iterable `ScanResult(lines, mask, codes, terminal)`; `unclosed_reason(terminal)` names the construct and its opening line
      `fence_scan` returns `(mask, unclosed_fence, unclosed_comment)`, so a third EOF-consuming construct has nowhere to report itself. Replace the boolean pair with a tagged terminal value naming the construct and its expected terminator, and route `unclosed_reason()` off it so every consumer keeps one wording.
      - The nine consumers unpack the tuple positionally, so this is an ABI change across the closure. Do it as ONE change with a corpus differential, not per consumer.
      - DESIGN-REVIEW ADOPTED [high]: return a NON-ITERABLE `ScanResult` with named `mask` / `terminal` fields rather than a wider tuple, so every one of the nine positional unpacks fails LOUDLY at the call instead of silently reading a truthy terminal object as a bool. Expose a `require_closed()` projection for the producer, the gates and the mutating tools. Do not retain the booleans and do not add a fourth tuple element.
      - DESIGN-REVIEW ADOPTED [medium]: a literal expected-terminator STRING cannot represent the seven end rules -- type 1 ends on a case-insensitive closing tag from the WHOLE `script|pre|style|textarea` set rather than the tag that opened it, and types 6-7 end BEFORE a blank line that must stay unmasked and still update container/paragraph state. Store a tagged `end_rule` for the machine plus a human-facing terminator description for the message.
      - ATOMICITY IS THE POINT, and the first cut of the §42/§43 split got it wrong: adding EOF-consuming constructs while `scripts/format-md-tables.py:139` still takes the mask alone would let that MUTATING tool rewrite a document whose mask erases to EOF, with lint Check 17 shelling out to its `--check` to bless the commit. Every mask-only consumer migrates in THIS section (Codex design review, [high]).
      - Acceptance: a balanced and an EOF-unclosed fixture for EVERY supported block type, each proving the producer REFUSES rather than publishing an empty node.
- [x] Add CommonMark HTML block types 1-5, which are the EOF-consuming ones -- plus type 6, driven by `HTML_BLOCK_RULES` copied from the markdown-it-py oracle
      `<script>` / `<pre>` / `<style>` / `<textarea>`, a processing instruction, a declaration and CDATA run to their terminator or EOF. `fence_scan` enters HTML state only for `<!--`. Measured 2026-08-11: **0** such openers outside a fence corpus-wide, so this masks nothing today and is insurance -- which is precisely why it must not ship without the contract above.
      - The rules are taken from the DIFFERENTIAL ORACLE rather than paraphrased from the spec, because parity with it is the acceptance bar: `markdown_it/rules_block/html_block.py:15` `HTML_SEQUENCES`. Types 1-5 include their terminator LINE in the block; the existing leaf-dies-with-container depth rule carries over unchanged (the oracle's own `sCount < blkIndent` break is that same rule).
- [x] Decide type-6 blocks on evidence, not on today's corpus -- per-line kind codes with two projections: `.mask` (structural + the table formatter) hides them, `.prose_mask()` (reflow only) shows them
- [x] Type 7 (a complete tag alone on a line) was WITHDRAWN here and SHIPPED in §46 -- four rounds, five [high] findings, zero live reach; the decision and its evidence are below
      The 62 known tag names (`<details>` among them) end at a blank line, so they cannot cause EOF erasure. §39 excluded them because masking would hide 30 live prose lines from the reflow lint while fixing nothing. The reviewer's counter stands: one boolean mask cannot serve a structural consumer and a prose consumer at once.
      - ADOPTED: a per-line block KIND with explicit CONSUMER PROJECTIONS, not a global boolean and not an external refusal gate -- an external gate cannot bind a direct builder or library call that never ran it (Codex design review, [high]).
      - The projection split is NOT structural-vs-prose. `format-md-tables.py` is not a prose consumer: it recognises and REWRITES pipe rows, and CommonMark reads a `|` row inside a type-6 block as raw HTML rather than a table, so letting it inspect type 6 opens a new literal-content rewrite class. Structural graph readers AND the table formatter exclude type 6; `todo-reflow.py` gets its own deliberately tested policy.
      - TYPE 7 WAS IN SCOPE AND WAS WITHDRAWN, on measurement, after four review rounds found five [high] defects in it and none anywhere else in the section. Its rule needs the complete-tag grammar, whose repeated attribute group makes CPython's engine backtrack: the regex reached **1,225 MB RSS** on a 12 MB line, and on ambiguity-inducing input it is CPU-superlinear far below any length bound (**0.53s at 6 KB, 6.19s at 20 KB, >19s at 64 KB**).
      - THREE REPAIRS WERE TRIED AND EACH FAILED DIFFERENTLY, which is the evidence that the shape was wrong rather than the code: (1) a length bound above which rule 7 was skipped -- FAIL-OPEN, a 4,125-char tag stopped being recognised and published the `## 99.` after it; (2) a hand-written linear recogniser -- three consecutive rounds found real divergences (ASCII-only whitespace, then the ambiguous run after `=`, then a greedy value failing later), because the reference resolves those by GLOBAL backtracking and a local scanner cannot; (3) the real regex bounded to 64 KiB with the scanner above it -- the threshold is itself a fail-open seam AND the bound was measured on benign input, so the CPU path survived it.
      - MEASURED LIVE REACH: **0**. Four corpus lines match type 7 and only type 7, and all four are inside a fenced block, so the rule never sees them. Withdrawing it left the markdown-it-py corpus differential at 0/281 -- the same number as shipping it.
      - INFERRED, and flagged as inference: any implementation that is both exact and cheap needs a non-backtracking matcher (a DFA/NFA state set), which is a different tool than this scan has. If it is revisited, that is the bar; do not spend a fifth round on a local scanner or a length threshold, both of which are now disproven.
      - The other six types ship: 1-5 are EOF-consuming (the actual erasure risk) and type 6 is the live `<details>` case, and every one of them is a cheap anchored pattern with no nested quantifier.
- [/] Model MULTI-LINE link reference definitions, the one bound section 39 states rather than solves -- attempted and REVERTED on measurement; **operator-gated**: the repair needs a backtracking block parser
      `_LINK_REF_DEF_RE` (`scripts/todo-graph/cache_schema.py`) clears paragraph state for the SINGLE-LINE form, so `[foo]: /url` no longer blocks the list after it. CommonMark also allows the destination and title to sit on FOLLOWING lines, and deciding where such a definition ENDS needs content-dependent parsing that section 39's three-flag block model does not have.
      - Consequence if unmodelled, which is why it is filed rather than shrugged at: `[foo]:` / `  /url` / `  "title"` / `10. item` / an indented fence is a list-contained fence to CommonMark, while the scan masks NOTHING and its `- [ ] ...` content reaches the item index as real structure (Codex adversarial, section 39 round 5, [medium]).
      - STRONGER THAN FIRST RECORDED: §39's post-commit review reproduced the fenced `- [x] Fake -> \`fake_fn()\`` being emitted by `extract_stamped_items` as a SHIPPED item, not merely indexed -- so the stub-behind-stamp resolver would chase a symbol that only exists in documentation (Codex adversarial, §39 post-commit, [high]).
      - Measured 2026-08-11: **0** link reference definitions of ANY form under `todo/`, so this is insurance, not a live defect -- and it is exactly the "measured zero" premise section 36 was burned by, so it gets an owner instead of a comment.
      - ESCALATED AGAIN BY §45, which made the stale stack load-bearing for SECTION IDENTITY rather than only for masking. `## 1. One` / `-` / `  [foo]:` / `    /url` / `lazy` / a TAB-indented `## 2. Two` + `- [x]`: the destination line reads as paragraph text, so `lazy` becomes a lazy continuation and holds the list open past where CommonMark closes it (oracle `bullet_list_open` map is [1,4]). §45's leaf projection then strips the tab against that stale stack and publishes section 2 AND a stamped item the oracle does not have -- and in a file whose Implementation Order lists rows 1 and 2 that fabricated heading SUPPRESSES the `orphan-io-row` refusal the physical-line walk used to raise, so a document validation previously refused now passes (Codex adversarial, §45 post-ship, [high]).
      - CONTROL that isolates it to the multi-line form: with the destination on the SAME line (`[foo]: /url`) the §45 walks agree with markdown-it exactly. A third line-at-a-time repair was drafted for §45 and NOT applied -- `_LINK_REF_DEF_RE`'s own comment records two prior attempts, the second measuring worse (509 -> 524 divergent of 2,000 generated documents) -- so the backtracking requirement this item already states is unchanged, only its blast radius is larger.
      - Acceptance: the shape above masks its fence and indexes no item; a differential over the corpus stays nil; the reciprocal pointer at `_LINK_REF_DEF_RE` names this item.
      - ATTEMPT 1, 2026-08-12, REVERTED ON ITS OWN MEASUREMENT. A three-state machine (label seen -> want destination -> want optional title, abandoned on a blank line) was written and measured against markdown-it-py on the shapes it targets: **509 divergent of 2,000** generated link-reference documents BEFORE, **524 AFTER**. It made the class it was for worse, so it was reverted rather than tuned. OBSERVED: both numbers, from the same harness, with the corpus differential nil in both directions.
      - WHY IT IS WORSE, so a second attempt does not rediscover it: whether `[foo]:` opens a definition or is a paragraph is NOT decidable line-by-line. A following `[bar]:` is simultaneously a plausible DESTINATION for the first label and a plausible new LABEL, and a single-pass model must commit before it can know. CommonMark parses the definition as a unit WITH BACKTRACKING. INFERRED, and flagged as inference: any line-at-a-time state machine has this shape, so the next attempt needs backtracking or it is the same failure again.
      - The bound is now STATED AT THE CODE (`_LINK_REF_DEF_RE`, `cache_schema.py`) with the measurement and the reason, rather than being a comment that only says "single-line only". 0 corpus instances, so nothing live is wrong today.
- [/] Reconcile the lazy-continuation / nested-container interaction, the second residual class -- BLOCKED on a parser with backtracking (three attempts reverted on measurement); operator-gated, see the sub-bullets
      §39's differential leaves exactly two divergences from CommonMark, and this is the one that is NOT link definitions. Shape: a checklist item, a MORE-indented nested item, a DEDENTED `- child`, then an indented fence -- e.g. `   - [ ] s` / `     - [ ] s` / `    - child` / `     ` + a fence. markdown-it-py ends both lists and reads the tail as one root indented code block; `fence_scan` keeps a container alive through the lazy line and opens a fence there, so it masks a line CommonMark does not and reports `unclosed_fence=True`.
      - Consequence: a FALSE fence hides real structure after it, and the terminal flag makes `build.py` REFUSE a valid document -- the fail-closed-on-legal-input direction, which is the worse of the two.
      - Measured 2026-08-11: **4 of 1,679,616** generated 4-line documents (0.0002%), and **0** corpus instances. An attempted fix (excluding any 4-column-indented line from lazy continuation) was REVERTED because it traded these 4 for 704 -- indentation alone does not separate a genuine lazy continuation from an indented code block at the fallback level, so this needs the real rule, not a threshold.
      - Acceptance: the four shapes above match markdown-it-py, the 4-line differential returns 0 in this class, and the corpus differential stays nil.
      - A SECOND principled attempt also measured worse -- **4 -> 5023** -- and was reverted (run log 20260811-151437, 18:29:20: "Both principled attempts at (b) measure worse (4->704, 4->5023), so it is the architectural limit"). OBSERVED: the measurement and the revert. NOT PRESERVED: the attempt's actual mechanism -- the run report truncates command bodies at ~200 chars and the metrics record carries none, so it cannot be recovered. INFERRED, and flagged as inference: it was almost certainly the round-7 reviewer's own recommendation, "resolve block precedence for the residual before shipping, using the CommonMark oracle rather than an indentation-only threshold" (`.claude/overnight/reviews/20260811-181740-adversarial.out:111`), which is what the run set out to test in the turn immediately after receiving that review.
      - WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER: without it, a later worker reads the reviewer's recommendation as the unexplored path. It is not -- it was tried and measured a thousand times worse than the indentation threshold it was meant to replace. Do not spend a fourth attempt rediscovering that. If the block-precedence route is revisited, treat 5023 as its baseline and say what is different this time.
      - PROCESS FINDING, filed here because this is where it bites: attempts 1 and 3 carry their mechanism into this body and are therefore actionable; attempt 2 reached only the ship stamp as a bare number. A revert's LESSON belongs in the owning section's item body, not only in the summary line -- the stamp is a report, this body is what the next worker reads.
      - §39 TRIED to remove the consequence and REVERTED it: suppressing `unclosed_fence` after any lazy continuation also suppressed GENUINE unclosed fences, publishing an EOF-erased document instead of refusing it. Per-fence scoping does not rescue it, because the divergent shape and a genuine one present identically to this model. So §39 reports the flag honestly and takes the trade fail-CLOSED -- a false refusal is loud, rare and fixed by reformatting; a false accept silently erases every heading, item, row and stamp past the opener.
- [/] Consider whether the section walks should read container-stripped lines -- CONSIDERED AND ANSWERED NO; the real repair is one shared section-context projection, owned by §43
      `SECTION_HEADING_RE` is anchored on the physical line, so a `## N.` indented six spaces inside a list item is a heading to CommonMark and invisible to the producer. Measured: 0 corpus instances.
      - IMPLEMENTED, THEN REVERTED ON REVIEW -- and what was reverted is the APPROACH, not just the code. `_walk_section_headings` was given `ScanResult.stripped` while every sibling walk kept reading physical lines, which desynchronised them: a root `## 1.`, a list-contained `## 2.` and an indented `[x]` item made the heading walk emit sections 1 and 2 while `_walk_stamped_items` missed the nested heading and filed the item under section 1 (Codex adversarial, §42, [high]).
      - That is the "two walks disagree about what a heading is" drift §36-§39 exist to end, so ONE container-aware walk is not an improvement on all of them being equally blind. Filing shipped-work evidence under the wrong section is worse than not seeing the heading at all.
      - The `stripped` array was removed from `ScanResult` with the wiring: its only consumer was this walk, and retaining a string reference per line tripled the scanner's per-line storage (a 4 MiB newline-only document held 4,194,305 entries per array) for a projection nothing could safely consume (Codex adversarial, §42, [medium]).
      -> XREF: [`TODO-06 §43`](#43-finish-the-one-contract-consolidation-across-the-consumer-closure) -- owns the real repair, one parsed section-context projection consumed by the heading, item, IO-row and XREF walks together (item: "Finish the one-contract consolidation §39 started, in the five places it did not reach").

## 43. Finish the One-Contract Consolidation Across the Consumer Closure

> **Spawned-by:** §42 (split)

Split out of §42 on 2026-08-11, verbatim, with the original item bodies and XREFs intact. §42 was filed as one section over two surfaces and `section-manifest.py` returned `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (10 work items; 6 subsystems; ABI impact)`. The seam is real rather than administrative: §42 owns `fence_scan`'s BLOCK MODEL and the terminal value it publishes, while this section owns the CONSUMERS of that contract -- the places where a second grammar, a second code-span state machine, a second exit-code convention or a transformed cell means the "one contract" claim is not yet true. Nothing here changes what the scanner decides; everything here changes who is allowed to decide it independently.

NARROWED 2026-08-12, before any code: `section-manifest.py` returned `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (9 files; 8 subsystems)` over the six substantive items this section carried, and the verdict is right -- they are four independent surfaces sharing only a file. The three that change what the SCANNER decides moved out verbatim, with their bodies and XREFs intact: inline span precedence and the Inputs table cell to §44, the container phase's cost and its erasure to §45, HTML block type 7 to §46. What stays here is what the title always named -- the CONSUMERS that still hold a second grammar, a second code-span state machine or a second exit-code convention.

> **Split override (structured waiver, accepted 2026-08-12):** the residue still scores `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (9 files; 8 subsystems)` on FILE COUNT, and it is overridden rather than split a second time. Estimate: 9 files (6 production, 3 test), 4 subsystems, ~10 tests, ~200K context. Every edit is the same edit -- DELETE a duplicate, ADOPT the rule `cache_schema` already publishes -- so the per-file diff is one regex, one state machine, one exit-code branch or one stderr envelope. The deliverable is a NEGATIVE invariant over the UNION of those files, and the grep-level control that must FAIL when a second grammar reappears has no meaning inside a half. The one item that needed NEW code rather than adoption left for §45; that move, not this waiver, is what returned the section to one worker context.

- [x] Pass the gate-exclusion set INTO the Check 7 helper, not just over its output
      §39 made Check 7 honour the commit-gate exclusion set by filtering the helper's stdout, which reaches its FINDINGS and not its coverage/population accounting. An excluded TODO that moves the stamped-symbol population still makes the helper return rc 7, and `scripts/lint.sh` still blocks the unrelated commit -- the exact unattended-worktree wedge the set exists to prevent (Codex adversarial, §39 round 7, [medium]).
      - §39 narrowed the banner to what is true rather than over-claiming a third time. The real fix is `STUB_LINT_EXCLUDED` (or equivalent) consumed by `scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py` before both finding generation and population accounting.
      - THE BASELINE IS THE HARD PART, and filtering nodes without touching it just moves the wedge. `scripts/lint/stub-lint-baseline.json` records GLOBAL `{resolved, total}` only, and `check_stub_behind_stamp.py:558` returns rc 7 whenever the live population differs from `total` at all -- so dropping excluded nodes before accounting GUARANTEES a `POPULATION SHRANK` refusal, while leaving accounting unexcluded preserves the original wedge exactly (Codex design review, §43, [high], verified at both lines).
      - So the baseline gains PER-OWNER contributions for `total` and `resolved`, asserted to sum to the global figures, and the runtime exclusion set is subtracted from the live view and the baseline view SYMMETRICALLY, keyed on the same canonical owner identity the findings are excluded by. Fail CLOSED on a malformed, duplicate, escaping or unknown identity -- an exclusion that silently matches nothing is the wedge with extra steps.
      - Acceptance: an rc-7 regression fixture where an excluded file moves the population and the commit is NOT blocked; a control where a NON-excluded file moves it and the commit IS blocked; per-owner contributions proven to sum to the global totals; an unknown exclusion identity refused rather than ignored; plus the banner restored to naming Check 7 wholly.
      - SHIPPED: `by_owner` records 66 owners summing exactly to the recorded 1,629 occurrences and 474 resolved, and `STUB_LINT_EXCLUDED` subtracts each named owner from the live view and the baseline view together. The fixtures run against the REAL cache and baseline rather than a synthetic tree -- the `rr` fixture tree resolves zero symbols, so no positive floor is satisfiable inside it and every run there refuses for a reason unrelated to exclusions.
      - Six fail-closed paths, each with a test: a stale identity, an escaping path, a duplicate entry, a baseline with no `by_owner`, per-owner sums that do not match the recorded totals, and an exclusion set large enough to drive the floor to zero -- which refuses rather than passing vacuously, because an exclusion may narrow this gate and may not switch it off (the last two came from the adversarial round, §43, [medium]).
- [x] Finish the one-contract consolidation §39 started, in the five places it did not reach
      §39's post-commit consistency review found the "one fence/newline/heading contract" claim still has five holes, none of them in code §39 edited -- which is why they are filed here rather than fixed under its stamp.
      - `scripts/todo-reachability.py:115` carries a SECOND, narrower heading grammar -- the drift §36-§39 exist to end. Its sibling claim, that `validate.py:1643` merely "retains a conflicting code-span state machine", was DISPROVEN by this section's design review and the item moved to §44: `validate.py`'s machine is the CORRECT one on mixed-length nesting and `cache_schema`'s is not, so deleting it here would have been a regression wearing a consolidation's clothes.
      - `scripts/todo-reachability.py:569-574` returns an unclosed document as an ordinary graph FINDING (rc 1 at `:713`) where §39 made `validate.py` `_refuse` it (rc 2 at `:316`). UNIFY THE CATEGORY, NOT THE CODE: `.claude/hooks/run_phase_guard.py:1721` blocks fixpoint on `reach_rc == 1` alone and lets every other nonzero fall through as clean, so RAISING this to rc 2 would hide an unclosed document from the completion gate -- and that caller is control-plane, which an unattended run may not edit (Codex design review, §43, [high], verified at that line).
      - rc 1 therefore STAYS and the unclosed document gets its own named category, distinguishable from an ordinary reachability finding; the rc-2 unification is filed for a control-plane-authorized change that migrates the caller in the same commit.
      - `scripts/lint.sh:1475` erases every formatter refusal, which is the exact defect Check 19 was hardened against in §41.
      - NOT HERE EITHER, moved 2026-08-12: the ONE SECTION-CONTEXT PROJECTION consumed by the four walks together is now §45's, because it CONSUMES the container phase §45 repairs. `cache_schema` exposes no per-line container view today (§42 published one and removed it in the same section, `cache_schema.py:1276-1283`), so this is new code rather than an adoption -- and exposing that view to all four walks BEFORE §45's indent check would spread §45's erasure defect from one walk to four.
      - FOUR COPIES, not two, and the fourth is CONTROL-PLANE. `cache_schema.SECTION_HEADING_RE` (0-3 space indent), `scripts/todo-reachability.py:106` and `.claude/hooks/sequencer_triage.py:55` (both column-0; the last uses `\s+` where the others use one space) and `scripts/todo-section-order.py:68` (column-0, bounded at 9 digits). The sequencer copy is under `.claude/hooks/`, which an unattended run may not edit, so it is FILED here rather than fixed (Codex consistency, §42, [medium]).
      - ALL FOUR feed an UNBOUNDED digit run to `int()`, which CPython refuses over 4,300 digits, so an over-long heading raises ValueError out of the producer AND out of the fixpoint gate. §42 bounded two of them to `\d{1,9}` and REVERTED both: bounding the MATCH makes the heading stop delimiting a section, so every item after it is silently re-attributed to the previous one -- trading a loud crash for exactly the quiet misattribution this file exists to prevent (Codex adversarial, §42, [high]). The repair is to MATCH and REPORT, in all four at once.
      - ONE CLASSIFIER, NOT ONE REGEX, and it returns a TAGGED result rather than a sentinel. `section_headings[].n` is pinned to an integer 0-65535 with `additionalProperties: false` (`schema/cache.schema.json:179-193`), so no scalar can stand for "over-long" -- and dropping the heading re-parents its items, which is the defect being fixed (Codex design review, §43, [high]). The classifier lands in `cache_schema` and is re-exported through `todo_fence` for the gate-facing callers.
      - Per-caller behaviour follows what each caller already does with an unrepresentable heading: a GATE reports it visibly (`todo-section-order.py:69` already does exactly this and is the reference), and the PRODUCER refuses fatally and preserves the previous cache byte-identically, the same class of refusal an unclosed document gets.
      - THREE of the four, not four: `.claude/hooks/sequencer_triage.py:55` is control-plane and an unattended run may not edit it, so the fourth copy is a NAMED RESIDUAL with its own follow-up rather than a claim this section quietly fails to meet (Codex design review, §43, [medium], accepted).
      - Acceptance: one classifier consumed by the three editable call sites, with the residual named; an over-long heading is REPORTED (not dropped, not crashed) with a fixture pinning both the report and the section boundaries around it; a control proving the bounded-match variant re-parents the following item; and an AST-level allowlist test that FAILS when a local heading matcher reappears outside the canonical module -- a grep control is evaded by a composed regex.
      - NOT HERE ANY MORE: `scripts/format-md-tables.py:139` taking the mask without the terminal state moved to §42, because that tool MUTATES and §42 adds the EOF-consuming constructs -- leaving its adoption one section behind would let it rewrite a document whose mask erases to EOF while lint Check 17 blesses the commit (Codex design review on §42, [high]). §43 keeps only the half that is not load-bearing for the new constructs: the refusal reaching the lint summary.
      - Check 17's envelope is pinned, not merely un-silenced: `--check` uses rc 1 for VIOLATIONS and an uncaught `MaskUnavailable` also exits 1, so surfacing stderr alone still counts a crash as zero violations (Codex design review, §43, [medium]). rc 0 is accepted only with no findings, rc 1 only with at least one well-formed violation record, and every other combination is an error that prints the formatter's own diagnostic.
      - Acceptance: one heading classifier across the editable consumers with the control-plane residual named, the same unclosed-document CATEGORY in every consumer, and a lint fixture proving both that a formatter refusal reaches the summary and that an rc-1 crash with no well-formed record is not counted as clean.
      - SHIPPED, and the count was FIVE editable copies, not three: the AST allowlist test found a sixth grammar in `scripts/todo-staged-check.py:277` that no hand-written inventory in §42 or §43 had listed, which is the argument for checking the AST rather than a list. `cache_schema.classify_heading` + `heading_report` are the rule; `todo_fence` re-exports both; `todo-reachability.py`, `todo-section-order.py` (which also lost `_OVERLONG_SECTION_RE`), `todo-staged-check.py` and both `build.py` walks consume them.
      - AND THE BOUNDARY RULE HAD TO MOVE WITH IT, which this section shipped wrong first: widening the START rule to CommonMark's 0-3 indent while every consumer still ENDED a section on a column-0 `## ` desynchronised the walks. Reproduced from the adversarial round -- `todo-reachability` counted an indented section's items in BOTH bodies, and the MUTATING `todo-section-order.reorder` relocated an indented `## Notes` block along with the section above it, which `parse` is written to refuse. `cache_schema.is_h2` is now the single boundary rule and both reproductions are pinned as fixtures (Codex adversarial, §43, [high]).
      - Check 17's envelope needed the same second pass: accepting rc 1 on ONE record-shaped line let a partial scan plus a traceback count as complete, because `format-md-tables.py:397` prints inside the file loop. It now requires EVERY stdout line to be an exact `would align: <path>` record AND stderr to be empty (Codex adversarial, §43, [medium]).
