## 44. The Inline Scanner Honours CommonMark Precedence, and the Table Cell Reaches It Unmodified

> **Spawned-by:** §43 (split)

Split out of §43 on 2026-08-12. The first two items live on the INLINE surface -- `_scan_code_spans` and the XREF-clause parser that consumes its mask -- and both are LATENT at 0 live-corpus incidence, so one differential harness and one contract decision (what a code span's boundary is, and what remainder makes a cell malformed) answers both.

A third item arrived the same day from §43's design review, which REVERSED the direction of a consolidation §43 was about to make: `validate.py`'s private code-span machine is the one that agrees with markdown-it-py on mixed-length nesting, so it may only be retired AFTER the canonical scanner is repaired here. That also removes §44's dependency on §43 -- the repair leads, the consolidation follows.

- [x] Make the Inputs TABLE cell reach the shared parser with its span boundaries intact
      `_walk_inputs_xrefs` transforms the cell before parsing it -- `_cell_code_span` unwraps a single-backtick span, and a caller-side strip removes one period or colon after a closing backtick -- so the boundary the shared rule needs is gone by the time the rule runs. The bullet surface parses the raw text and therefore disagrees with the table on shapes neither surface should treat differently.
      - Measured divergences (Codex adversarial + consistency, §40, both [medium]): after `` `path §7` `` a `)`, comma, semicolon or `](#x)` link tail is dropped by the bullet and folded into the table's marker; and a cell whose span uses a LONGER delimiter run emits an edge as a bullet and no edge at all as a table row, because the unwrap only knows single backticks.
      - LATENT: **0** live corpus rows carry either shape, and §40's live-corpus invariant is clean, so nothing shipped is wrong today. It is filed rather than fixed because the repair is a CONTRACT decision -- what remainder makes a cell malformed -- and that contract already carries findings from §33, §37 and §40; widening a two-character allowlist would be the fourth patch to the same boundary rather than an answer.
      - SHIPPED: both pre-transforms DELETED (`_CELL_SPAN_TAIL_RE` and `_cell_code_span`, now dead code, removed) and the raw cell reaches `parse_xref_clause`, which already locates a marker inside a span and stops at the delimiter. Nothing replaced them: the unwrap was imitating by hand what §40 had made the parser do, so removing the imitation is what makes the surfaces agree.
      - `cache_schema.classify_remainder` states the rule by CLASS -- exhausted / terminal / marker / markup / xref / prose -- and `inputs_clause_ok` applies it to both callers. The surfaces differ on ONE stated axis: a table CELL is a bounded field (`whole=True`), a BULLET is a sentence that legitimately continues, which is house style. `REMAINDER_MARKUP` is refused on BOTH, because a clause cut inside a link bound only part of the destination.
      - Measured bullet-vs-table disagreement over the 13 shapes: **5 before, 1 after**, and the one remaining is that stated axis. The `)`/`,`/`;`/`](#x)` tails were folded into the table's marker (`§7)`, `§7,`) and are not any more; sub-test 44e asserts parity per tail class and 40f's control was INVERTED rather than dropped.
      -> XREF: [`TODO-06 §40`](#40-an-inputs-section-marker-keeps-its-closing-backtick-so-one-dependency-is-two-edges) -- the section that consolidated the marker rule and left this surface unreached (item: "Normalise the section marker on BOTH XREF subtrees, in the shared parser, and prove it with a per-clause differential").
- [x] Honour CommonMark's leftmost-opener precedence when delimiter runs of different lengths nest
      `_scan_code_spans` closes whichever pending run length it meets first, so an outer one-backtick span containing a PAIRED two-backtick run is read as the inner pair instead of the outer span. CommonMark closes the earliest active opener, and markdown-it renders one span there.
      - CONSEQUENCE, and why it is filed here rather than left as a conformance note: §40 made the mask decide where an XREF clause ENDS, so a mis-marked span lets the iterator publish the span's own documented contents as a real graph edge. Reproduced: one stamp line emits two rows, the second naming a file the author only quoted, and the outer clause loses its item name.
      - PRE-EXISTING, not a §40 regression -- verified by replaying the HEAD producer over the same line, which emits the same two clauses by a different route. The escaped-run branch has the same shape: it shortens a run and immediately closes a pending opener with the remainder.
      - Measured: **0** live corpus lines carry a mixed-length nested run today, which is why it is an item rather than a stop-ship. The repair belongs to the scan's pending-opener bookkeeping, under §39's bounded-memory constraint (it may not index every run), so it is a scanner change with its own differential rather than a parser fix.
      - SHIPPED: the pending-opener map is gone. An opener now consumes FORWARD to the first run of equal FULL length, so the leftmost opener wins; state is a count per distinct run length, built LAZILY on the first opener that never closes, which is what preserves §40j's `stop` bound (an up-front census would read the whole line and reinstate the 0.0008s -> 3.88s cliff). Measured after: 40j ratio 11246x against a floor of 20; a pathological 600-distinct-length line 0.0156s; a 4 MiB dense line 0.637s.
      - The "0 live lines" estimate in this item was WRONG, and only a real oracle showed it. Against markdown-it-py the pre-repair scan diverged on **11** corpus lines (10 precedence, 1 escaped-run), not 0; after the repair, **0** on span boundaries. Fixture battery 26/26. The graph is byte-identical across all 232 nodes for this change, so the divergences were latent -- the estimate was right about impact and wrong about incidence.
      - A CLOSER IS MEASURED AT FULL RUN LENGTH, an opener at its escape-shortened one: `` ``x\``` `` is no span, while `` \```x`` `` is one. The old scan shortened the run and let the remainder close, fabricating a span; sub-test 44b pins both directions.
      -> XREF: [`TODO-06 §40`](#40-an-inputs-section-marker-keeps-its-closing-backtick-so-one-dependency-is-two-edges) -- the section whose review found it (item: "Normalise the section marker on BOTH XREF subtrees, in the shared parser, and prove it with a per-clause differential"); §40 consumes the mask and does not own the scan.
- [x] Retire `validate.py`'s private code-span machine, against markdown-it-py rather than against itself
      Arrived from §43 on 2026-08-12 by its design review, which held that `validate.py:1643` `_code_span_segments` was the CORRECT implementation and `cache_schema._scan_code_spans` the wrong one. Half right: it IS correct on mixed-length nesting, which is why the repair had to lead and the deletion follow. It is not correct overall.
      - THE PLANNED ACCEPTANCE WAS WRONG AND MEASUREMENT REVERSED IT. Against markdown-it-py 3.0.0 over all 43,891 backticked corpus lines, `validate.py` diverged on **394** and `cache_schema` on **11**, so proving parity against `validate.py` would have locked the 394 in. It applied its backslash test at the CLOSER as well as the opener; CommonMark escapes openers only, so in a Win32-native repo whose canonical paths end in a separator, `` `C:\Users\` `` never closed and every link and anchor after it was read out of a span it thought still open.
      - SHIPPED: repaired the shared scan first, proved it at markdown-it parity, then deleted the 83-line private machine to a one-line alias of `cache_schema.code_span_segments`. `iter_code_spans` publishes `(start, inner_start, inner_end, end)` because `_rendered_inline_text` needs SEGMENTS, not a blanked mask.
      - Verified corpus-neutral: `validate.py --repo-root .` output is byte-identical before and after (10/10 checks, 0 failures), so the 394 were latent rather than firing. Sub-test 44d keeps the retired algorithm as a fixture the shared rule must DISAGREE with on the closer-escape shapes, with a control shape they must still agree on.
- [x] Bound the section-marker grammar, because the remainder CLASS cannot classify what the parser already ate
      Not a listed item; it became one because item 1's acceptance is unreachable without it. `_XREF_SECTION_RE` was `§\S+`, running to the next space, so `-> XREF: a.md §5, and more` bound `§5,` and `a.md §1](#x)` bound a fragment of a link -- and `classify_remainder` then reported `exhausted` for text that plainly had a remainder. Measured on the live graph: **38 edges across 14 forms** carrying a value no resolver matches (`§5,` x8, `§3,` x6, `§3:` x2, `§1.`), all silently unresolvable.
      - The grammar is digits plus the joins a multi-section reference uses, INCLUDING both typographic dashes, so `§10-13`, `§1-§5` and `§3,§4` still pass through byte-for-byte. That was §40's stated reason for rejecting this grammar (ranges truncating at the first number) and 40c still enforces it; what changed is where the marker ENDS, not what it may contain. A trailing separator is trimmed and handed back as a remainder.
      - `REMAINDER_MARKER` exists because tightening alone DROPPED two live edges: `TODO-C §"Tier 1"` and `TODO-03 §*` are real dependencies whose marker is not a section number, and refusing the clause lost the destination as well. A remainder opening with the section glyph keeps the edge and binds no section, which is the honest answer.
      - Net corpus effect: 38 values repaired, 1 wildcard normalised to None, and 1 stamp edge dropped by `build.py:1760`'s pre-existing "no section marker, no edge" policy meeting a now-correct parse.
- [x] Refuse an XREF lead that starts INSIDE a code span, in the iterator rather than by accident
      Found by sub-test 40e failing after the marker was bounded, and it is the most important thing this section learned. Nothing had ever REFUSED a `-> XREF:` written inside a code span: `§\S+` ran to the next space and happened to swallow the span containing it, so the [high] fabricated-edge defect §40 fixed was being held shut by a side effect of a greedy regex. Bounding the marker removed the side effect and the defect reopened.
      - `iter_xref_clauses` now skips a lead whose START is masked. The test is on the start only and matching stays on the RAW line: a mask fills a span WITH whitespace and the lead consumes its own trailing whitespace, so searching the mask directly let a lead run through a blanked span and bind the closing delimiter as its target (caught by 40b/40h/40i going empty on the first attempt).
      - 40e's control was rewritten to mutate the MASK rather than the marker regex, because that is where the protection now lives; mutating the marker no longer reopens the defect, so a control that kept doing so would have silently stopped testing anything.
- [x] Keep a spanned marker's trimmed punctuation as span content, and stop the bounded mask allocating the whole line
      Both from the post-ship review, and the adversarial and consistency legs reported the first INDEPENDENTLY. `marker_cut` is measured against the marker MATCH while the spanned path's cursor is the SPAN end -- a different coordinate -- so `end = p - marker_cut` left `clause.end` on the closing backtick. `` `a.md §7,` `` reported a one-backtick remainder, which the bullet tolerated and the bounded cell refused: the parity split this section exists to close, reopened by its own repair.
      - The perf leg found `_scan_code_spans` still building `list(s)`, which materialises the WHOLE line as soon as ANY span closes -- including the tail the bounded walk never scanned. Measured on a permitted 16 MiB line with one early span: 0.162s and ~147 MiB transient RSS against 14us with no span. Rebuilt from slices: 0.021s and 86 MiB peak. 40j could not see it because its fixture has no span before the bound, so the allocation never fired.
      - Both pinned by 44h, which compares bounded against unbounded on a line that HAS an early closed span, and asserts parity plus an empty remainder for every spanned tail.
- [x] Let a section-marker join carry whitespace, so a multi-section reference is not silently narrowed
      From the re-adversarial round, whose stated finding was WRONG and whose underlying defect was real. It reported that the spanned-marker fix "turns in-span suffixes into silently consumed clause content"; replaying the HEAD~1 producer shows the baseline already returned `rem=''` and `malformed=None` for `` `a.md §3, §7` ``, so the swallow is pre-existing and the fix changed only the VALUE. The rejection is recorded rather than the verdict.
      - The real defect it led to: the marker grammar accepted the `,` join but not the SPACE after it, so `§3,§4` matched whole while `§1, §2` stopped at `§1`. **29 live references** bound only their first section and dropped the rest. A narrowed dependency is worse than a wrong one because it looks clean, and nothing downstream re-checks a marker's shape.
      - A separator now continues the marker only when digits follow it, so a trailing `§5,` still ends at `§5` and hands the comma back. Repeated separators are allowed, because `§1--§8` is this repo's ASCII spelling of a range and the first cut of the fix narrowed it to `§1`. Pinned by 40c's passthrough list.
- [/] Retire the in-band `SPAN_END_MARK` sentinel now that the iterator publishes offsets -- BLOCKED on an owner section; no cross-TODO owner exists, so this is parked here rather than pointing at a target that would not take it
      `SPAN_END_MARK` is `"\x00"` written INTO the mask, and `cache_schema.py:870` justifies it as "a control character precisely because it cannot occur in a TODO line". That claim is FALSE: `todo/01-boot-platform/TODO-04-firmware-table-platform-inventory.md:305` contains a literal NUL. Probed live -- a NUL OUTSIDE any span survives into the mask and `_span_end_after` reads it as a fabricated span end.
      - NOT reachable today, which is why it is parked rather than fixed here: the one corpus NUL sits INSIDE a code span, so it is masked to a space and never becomes a false mark. It needs a NUL outside a span on a line that also carries a marker inside one.
      - The structural fix is to retire the sentinel for the `(start, end)` offsets `iter_code_spans` now publishes, which this section added. It is parked because it is a contract change across `parse_xref_clause`'s `masked=` parameter and two tests that monkeypatch `_span_end_after` (`test_build.sh:18464`) and `_mask_marking_span_ends` (40j), not a local edit -- and §44 already carries four shipped repairs.
- [/] Repair the `§"Tier 1"` XREF at `todo/02-kernel-core/TODO-22-environment-variables.md:587` -- BLOCKED on the owner of that stamped section; it is someone's Accepted stamp, not this section's to rewrite
      The stamp names `10-platform-services/TODO-C §"Tier 1"`, a TIER rather than a section number. Before §44 it published `target_section: '§"Tier'`, a value no resolver has ever matched; after §44 the marker grammar correctly declines it and `build.py:1760`'s existing policy drops the edge. Either way the dependency is not in the graph -- §44 changed it from silently broken to absent.
      - The repair is an authoring fix in that file (name a real section, or drop the marker and keep the bare target, which the graph WOULD bind). Filed rather than done because editing another section's Accepted stamp to make one's own numbers look better is exactly the move the review discipline forbids.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: the inline scanner's span boundaries survive the table cell"`

**Test checkpoint:** the raw cell reaches `parse_xref_clause` with the acceptance rule stated by remainder CLASS rather than a punctuation list, and 44e carries bullet-vs-table parity per tail class (40f's control INVERTED, since the behaviour it pinned was the defect); mask parity with markdown-it-py on mixed-run and escaped-run fixtures (44a/44b) with 40e's mutation control reproducing the fabricated edge; the shared rule must DISAGREE with the retired `validate.py` machine on the closer-escape fixtures and agree on an ordinary span (44d) -- the planned "prove they agree first" acceptance was reversed by measurement, because agreeing would have locked in that machine's 394 divergences; the corpus differential is NOT nil and is enumerated instead (39 section values repaired, 1 wildcard normalised, 1 stamp edge dropped by an existing policy, 0 unexplained); `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- 618/618 passed, 0 failed (sub-tests 44a-44h new; 40a/40c/40e/40f controls updated to the contract this section changed).

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: `cache_schema._scan_code_spans` rewritten to CommonMark leftmost-opener precedence over a new `iter_code_spans` offset iterator, with `code_span_segments` as its segment view.
> - `validate.py`'s 83-line private code-span machine is retired to a one-line alias; measured against markdown-it-py it diverged on 394 live corpus lines against the shared scan's 11, reversing this section's planned acceptance.
> - The Inputs table cell now reaches `parse_xref_clause` raw: both pre-transforms deleted, and `classify_remainder`/`inputs_clause_ok` state one acceptance rule for the bullet and table surfaces.
> - The section-marker grammar is bounded to section characters, repairing 38 live graph edges that carried an unresolvable value like `§5,`; `REMAINDER_MARKER` keeps the two edges whose marker is not a section number.
> - Downstream: `iter_xref_clauses` refuses a lead starting inside a code span -- that [high] defect had only ever been held shut by the greedy marker regex, and bounding the marker reopened it.
> - Two review rounds found six defects, five of them created by this section's own drafts; all fixed and pinned by 44g/44h, and the corpus effect is enumerated rather than claimed nil.
> - Scope boundary: the in-band `SPAN_END_MARK` sentinel and one corpus authoring defect are parked as `- [/]` items above, not fixed here.

## 45. The Container Phase Stops Being Quadratic, Stops Erasing, and Becomes the Section Context the Four Walks Share

> **Spawned-by:** §43 (split)

Split out of §43 on 2026-08-12. The first item moved verbatim: one item, two symptoms, ONE function -- `_match_containers` matches a blank line against arbitrarily many filled list containers without consuming input and without checking the container's content indent. The first symptom is O(N^2) work on an O(N)-byte document; the second is silently erased graph data. Both are PRE-EXISTING and §42 verified that at `f3ea34fd6`, so this is the container phase's own cost and indent model, not anything the block-kind work introduced.

The second item followed it here on the same day, out of §43's consolidation list, because ORDER is load-bearing rather than thematic: the shared section-context projection CONSUMES this phase's output, and publishing that output to all four producer walks before the indent check above is repaired would spread the erasure from one walk to four. §43 keeps every consolidation that adopts an EXISTING shared rule; this one has no rule to adopt yet (§42 published a per-line container view and removed it in the same section, `cache_schema.py:1276-1283`), so it is new code and it belongs on top of a repaired phase.

- [x] Blank-line container matching is QUADRATIC, and a size-valid TODO can stall every consumer
      `_match_containers` walks the whole open container stack for every line, and the blank-line branch matches arbitrarily many FILLED list containers without consuming input. `"- " * N + "x\n" + "\n" * N` is O(N) bytes and O(N^2) work: measured 2026-08-12 at **0.068s for 1,000 levels (3 KB), 0.276s for 2,000, 1.20s for 4,000** -- so a document far below the 16 MiB per-TODO ceiling stalls `build.py` and every migrated gate (Codex re-adversarial, §42 round 5, [high]).
      - PRE-EXISTING, and §42 verified that: the function is byte-identical to its §39 form, so this is the container phase's own cost model rather than anything the block-kind work introduced. Filed here because §43 already owns the container/grammar consolidation and this is the same walk.
      - The repair is stack metadata that resolves blank-line continuation without traversing every open container, or an explicit work budget. Acceptance: a scaling regression showing near-linear runtime as nesting and blank-line count double, plus the 4,000-level document under a fixed time bound.
      - SAME ROOT CAUSE, SECOND SYMPTOM -- an under-indented blank keeps a list-contained HTML leaf alive, and this one ERASES. `_match_containers` treats any spaces/tabs-only line as matching a filled list container without checking that its indent reaches the container's content indent, so `## 1. Root` / `- a` / an indented `<?pi` / an under-indented blank / `## 2. Real` + `- [x] Real item` masks section 2 and its item: markdown-it-py ends the block at the blank and exposes them, while the producer emits headings 1 and 3, no stamped item, and NO terminal error -- silently erased graph data rather than a refusal (Codex re-adversarial, §42 round 6, [high]).
      - PRE-EXISTING, and verified so rather than assumed: the identical divergence reproduces at `f3ea34fd6` (pre-§42) using `<!--`, which was already a container-alive HTML leaf. §42 widened the reach from one HTML type to five; it did not introduce the rule. A fenced block in the same shape agrees with the oracle in both versions, which is what isolates this to the HTML-leaf path.
      - Acceptance for this half: a markdown-it differential fixture with an under-indented blank followed by a real heading and item, for EACH substring-terminated HTML class, plus the comment control that proves the behaviour predates the section.
      - SHIPPED as two rules in `cache_schema.py`. The cost model is a `[first_unfilled, first_bq]` stack metadata pair maintained by `_open_containers` and `fence_scan`'s truncation, so `_match_containers:1898` answers a blank line in O(1) instead of walking the stack: 1.372s -> 0.0098s at 4,000 levels, and linear to 8,000. The indent check records `html_indent` = the base `_open_containers` now returns, and ends a substring-terminated block when the PHYSICAL line is blank and its indent falls below it -- markdown-it's `html_block` break on `sCount < blkIndent`. All five substring-terminated classes now agree with the oracle, with four falsifiers pinning the scope (a blank AT the content indent, a root-level block, a list-contained FENCE, and a bare `>` inside a blockquote-contained block, all unchanged).
- [x] ONE section-context projection, consumed by the four producer walks TOGETHER, on top of the repaired phase
      Moved here from §43 on 2026-08-12, before any code. All four walks are anchored on the PHYSICAL line today -- `_walk_section_headings` (`build.py:1078`), `_walk_stamped_items` (`build.py:1169`), `_walk_implementation_order` (`build.py:1406`) and `_walk_inputs_xrefs` (`build.py:1515`) -- and each consumes `scan_body`'s fence-mask-only `ScanResult`, whose `__slots__` deliberately carry no per-line container view (`cache_schema.py:1265-1310`).
      - §42 proved the failure mode by trying the narrow version: giving only `_walk_section_headings` the container-stripped view made it see a list-contained `## N.` that `_walk_stamped_items` did not, so an indented `[x]` item was filed under the PREVIOUS section (Codex adversarial, §42, [high]). Either all the walks share one notion of where a section starts, or none of them may be container-aware.
      - ORDER, not theme, is why it is here: the projection publishes exactly what the two items above repair, so shipping it first would hand every walk a view that erases what follows an under-indented blank, turning one consumer's defect into four.
      - Acceptance: a fixture with a root `## 1.`, a list-contained `## 2.` and an indented `[x]` item attributes the item to section 2 in every walk, with a control proving the physical-line producer attributes it to section 1.
      - SHIPPED as TWO published projections on `ScanResult`, not one, and the closure is FIVE walks rather than four. `views` strips MATCHED container prefixes and keeps the marker a line opens (the item regex, the IO rows and the XREF bullets match on it); `leaf_views` also consumes the openers, and heading classification everywhere reads that one. Both are one packed byte per line -- bit 7 the blockquote flag, bits 0-6 the character cut, `0x7F` an escape into a sparse map -- with the leaf array allocated only once some line diverges, and both returning `lines` itself when nothing does.
      - `_walk_unusable_headings` joined the closure on review: it is a fifth `classify_heading` consumer and the one that REFUSES an unrecordable number, so leaving it on physical lines would have turned that refusal into a silent omission for exactly the headings the other four had just started seeing. `_walk_stamps_xrefs` stays on physical lines on purpose -- it matches `STAMP_HEADER_RE` against the `>` marker itself and carries no section context.
      - The second projection came from the adversarial round: `views` alone left `- ## 2. Nested` -- a real heading to CommonMark -- invisible, so its item filed under the previous section and the over-long form `- ## 12345678901.` recorded no `unusable-heading` at all, failing OPEN. `_walk_stamped_items` also lost the `>` it used to reject blockquoted `[x]` lines on, so that fact is published as `in_blockquote(i)` instead of inferred from surviving text.
      - Corpus impact is nil and measured: 18,505 of 90,801 lines carry a container prefix and 9,336 sit inside a blockquote, yet 0 heading and 0 item classifications change, the built cache is byte-identical at 2,566,213 bytes and `producer_differential.py` reports 3,689 stamped refs both ways over both corpora.

## 46. CommonMark HTML Block Type 7 Needs a Non-Backtracking Matcher

> **Spawned-by:** §43 (split)

Split out of §43 on 2026-08-12, verbatim. It is one item and it is a section on its own because §42 spent four review rounds proving three cheap implementations wrong -- five [high] defects, all in this rule and none elsewhere in that section -- before withdrawing it. The hazard is the GRAMMAR (repeated attribute groups backtrack: 1,225 MB RSS at 12 MB, 6.19s at 20 KB), so the bar is a matcher that is exact and cheap by construction, not a fifth local scanner behind a threshold.

- [x] Recognise CommonMark HTML block type 7, which §42 withdrew rather than ship -- a 16-state NFA subset-constructed to a 24-state DFA at import, PROVED equivalent to the oracle rather than sampled against it
      A complete open or closing tag alone on a line opens a type-7 block, so a `## N.` or `- [x]` inside one is hidden by CommonMark and still indexed here. §42 tracked types 1-6 and withdrew this one after four review rounds found five [high] defects in it and none elsewhere in that section.
      - SHIPPED: `is_complete_tag_line` plus a 7th `HTML_BLOCK_RULES` row with `can_interrupt=False` and `END_ON_BLANK`, placed LAST so `<pre>` stays type 1 and `<details>` stays type 6. The five dead `_OPEN_TAG`/`_CLOSE_TAG`/`_ATTRIBUTE` constants -- the withdrawn regex, referenced nowhere -- are DELETED rather than left as a trap for the next implementer.
      - AND TYPE 7 IS HIDDEN FROM `todo-reflow.py`, reversing what §42's own comment predicted. A valid 95-char opener sits inside that tool's 78-138 hard-wrap band, so it joins the opener into the prose after it; type 6's NAME-anchored opener survives that join, but type 7 needs a complete tag ALONE on the line, so the block ceases to exist and the `## 99.` and `- [x]` it hid are republished as graph data. Reproduced, with a control that corrupts when the kind is made visible again.
      - The paragraph state passed to the opener is now the one INSIDE any container this line just opened: a bullet, `>` or `1.` marker closes the paragraph it interrupts, and `para` / `- <x>` / `  ## 99.` diverged from the oracle in all three forms until it did. That path was unreachable before type 7, the only `can_interrupt=False` row.
      - THE PROOF IS A DECISION PROCEDURE, NOT A SAMPLE, which is the design review's first [high]: both machines are finite, so `test_todo_fence.py` compiles the oracle's OWN live pattern string into an NFA, determinises it, and walks the PRODUCT to fixpoint -- 34 reachable states, no counterexample, i.e. equivalence over all of Unicode rather than up to a chosen length.
      - The finite alphabet is justified MECHANICALLY rather than by intuition: over every one of the 1,114,112 code points the shipped 14-class partition is BIJECTIVE with the oracle's own 14 atom signatures, so one representative per class provably stands in for all of them. A hand-picked alphabet is exactly what the three earlier attempts had.
      - CONTROLS THAT MUST FIRE, AND DID: re-folding the close-tag prefix into the name state yields `'</>'` as the shortest counterexample, and dropping wide whitespace from the unquoted class yields `'<A\tA=\x85>'` -- the NBSP-class ambiguity that NONE of the hand-written boundary cases caught, which is the whole argument for the product proof.
      - The design review's [high] was real and is fixed: the character after `</` must be an ASCII LETTER, so `_Q_CLOSE_START` is its own state; folding it into `_Q_CNAME` accepts `</>` and `</9a>`.
      - `\s` in Python's `re` under str patterns IS `str.isspace()` -- verified over all code points, zero disagreements -- and 19 code points are simultaneously `\s` AND legal unquoted content, one of the two ambiguities the state set exists for. This is NOT `_is_blank`'s question (spaces and tabs only) and the two predicates stay separate.
      - MEASURED LIVE REACH, unchanged from §42: **0** type-7 lines across all 281 corpus files, and the markdown-it-py differential stays 0/281.
      - DO NOT re-try the three approaches §42 disproved: a LENGTH BOUND above which the rule is skipped (fail-open -- a 4,125-char tag stopped being recognised and published the `## 99.` after it); a HAND-WRITTEN linear recogniser (three consecutive rounds found real divergences, because the reference resolves whitespace/value ambiguity by GLOBAL backtracking); and the real regex bounded to 64 KiB with that scanner above it (the threshold is itself a fail-open seam, and the bound was measured on benign input so the CPU path survived).
      - The hazard is the grammar, not the code: repeated attribute groups make CPython's engine backtrack -- **1,225 MB RSS at 12 MB**, and CPU-superlinear at **0.53s/6 KB, 6.19s/20 KB, >19s/64 KB** on ambiguity-inducing input.
      - INFERRED, and flagged as inference: an implementation that is both exact and cheap needs a non-backtracking matcher (a DFA/NFA state set). That is the bar; a fifth local-scanner or threshold attempt is not.
      - Acceptance: mask parity with markdown-it-py on generated type-7 fixtures INCLUDING non-ASCII whitespace in every grammar position; a CPU-bound regression on the ambiguous-attribute shape; the live corpus differential stays 0/281.
      -> XREF: [`TODO-06 §42`](#42-html-blocks-join-the-tracker-and-the-terminal-contract-carries-the-block-kind) -- the section that added types 1-6 and withdrew this one (item: "Type 7 (a complete tag alone on a line) was WITHDRAWN here and SHIPPED in §46").
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: HTML block type 7 joins the tracker"`

**Test checkpoint:** mask parity with markdown-it-py on generated type-7 fixtures INCLUDING non-ASCII whitespace in every grammar position; a CPU-bound regression on the ambiguous-attribute shape that FAILS against a backtracking implementation; the live corpus differential stays 0/281; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`, `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

**Test checkpoint, AS MET** -- one criterion was met in a DIFFERENT axis than it was written in, and that is recorded rather than quietly re-scoped:

- Mask parity with markdown-it-py on generated type-7 fixtures, including non-ASCII whitespace in every grammar position: MET, and superseded by something stronger -- the product-automaton proof decides equivalence over ALL input, so the fixtures are now a regression net rather than the evidence.
- "A CPU-bound regression that FAILS against a backtracking implementation": MET AS A MEMORY-BOUND regression. §42's CPU figures (0.53s/6 KB, 6.19s/20 KB) could NOT be reproduced -- six candidate witness shapes were measured on 2026-08-12 and every one was linear in CPU -- so that witness is not in hand and no test claims it. What DOES reproduce is the memory amplification behind §42's other number: the withdrawn regex holds ~164 bytes of backtracking state per input byte (4 MB witness -> 656 MB RSS), so `test_build.sh` Test 41 runs both implementations on a 2 MB witness under one 256 MB `RLIMIT_AS` in SUBPROCESSES. The regex dies with `MemoryError`; the DFA answers in 0.09s. The control firing is asserted separately, so a test that stopped discriminating would fail rather than pass quietly.
- Live corpus differential 0/281 and 0 type-7 lines corpus-wide: MET, measured directly.
- `test_build.sh`, `test_todo_fence.py` (530 assertions), `test_format_md_tables.py` and `test-tooling.sh` all green: MET. Counts are restated at the review stamp rather than here, because the review round added assertions after this line was first written and a stale count is exactly the drift this block exists to avoid.

> **Notes:**
> - `is_complete_tag_line` (`cache_schema.py`) recognises CommonMark HTML block type 7 with a 16-state NFA subset-constructed to a 24-state DFA at import: a table index per character, no allocation, no input-dependent control flow.
> - It joins `HTML_BLOCK_RULES` as a 7th row, LAST and `can_interrupt=False`, so type 1-6 precedence and the no-interrupting-a-paragraph rule fall out of machinery §42 already built.
> - Type 7 is HIDDEN from `todo-reflow.py`, which §42 predicted it would not be: a 95-char opener sits in that tool's hard-wrap band, and joining prose onto it destroys a complete tag (type 6's name-anchored opener survives the same join), republishing the structure the block hid.
> - Equivalence is DECIDED, not sampled: the test determinises the oracle's own live pattern and walks the product automaton to fixpoint, over an alphabet proved bijective with the oracle's atom signatures across all code points.
> - Test 41 pins WHY type 7 was withdrawn: the old regex and the new DFA run on one 2 MB witness under one 256 MB `RLIMIT_AS`, in subprocesses; the regex dies, the DFA answers, and the control firing is its own assertion.
> - Live reach is 0 and the markdown-it-py differential stays 0/281, so this closes a correctness gap rather than changing output. The five dead `_OPEN_TAG`/`_ATTRIBUTE` regex constants are deleted.
> - Scope boundary: §42's two parked CommonMark residuals are untouched, and the residual type-6 case (the rewriter joins a long `<details ...>` opener, moving literal raw-HTML bytes but NOT exposing structure) is filed to §47; the type-7 form of it is FIXED here.

## 47. The Gate Consumers Adopt the Section-Context Projections the Producer Already Uses

> **Spawned-by:** §45 (review)
> **User impact:** a TODO that nests a `## N.` heading inside a list item is a real section to the producer and invisible to three gates, so its open items are filed under the PREVIOUS section and the fixpoint gate can report DONE over open work; the same section bypasses the section-count cap and the mandatory `Spawned-by` provenance check; and a link to it is reported as a dead anchor when it actually resolves.

§45 gave `build.py`'s five section-context walks the container-stripped projections, and measured 0 classification changes across 90,801 corpus lines -- but it moved only the PRODUCER. Three gates still classify headings from physical lines, so producer and enforcement now disagree about what a section is. Before §45 they agreed by being equally blind; that is the property this section restores, in the other direction.

> **Split override (structured waiver, accepted 2026-08-12):** the residue still scores `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (5 subsystems)` after the mutating half left for §48, and it is overridden rather than split again. Estimate: 6 files (3 production -- `todo-reachability.py`, `todo-staged-check.py`, `todo-graph/validate.py`; 3 test), 3 real subsystems, ~8 tests, ~200K context. Three of the five counted "subsystems" are the shared test surface every section of this file touches (`scripts/tests`, `scripts/todo-graph`, `scripts/test-tooling.sh`), so the verdict is scoring where the tests live rather than what the work is -- §48, which has ONE work item and cannot be split at all, scores 4 by the same rule. Every edit here is the same edit: DELETE a physical-line heading classifier, ADOPT the leaf projection `cache_schema` already publishes. The deliverable is a POSITIVE agreement invariant -- producer and all three gates return the same section set and the same item ownership over ONE shared fixture -- and that oracle has no meaning inside a half, because agreement between two of four parties proves nothing.

**Continuation waiver (review-spawn at depth 4, limit 3, accepted 2026-08-12).** `not_parkable`: §45 is the section being stamped and the fixpoint loop never revisits a DONE section, so a `- [/]` park there strands it; §43, the natural owner of the consumer closure, is stamped and closed; no open section can hold it. `severity_trend`: the post-ship round produced 1 high + 2 medium in this class, on a DISTINCT surface (gate consumers) rather than a deepening of the producer surface. `surface`: `scripts/todo-reachability.py`, `scripts/todo-staged-check.py`, `scripts/todo-graph/validate.py`.

- [x] `todo-reachability.py` classifies headings from the leaf projection, not physical lines
      `_sections` now takes the whole `ScanResult` and classifies starts and the `is_h2` boundary from `leaf_views`, skipping `in_blockquote` lines exactly as `build.py:1096` does (`scripts/todo-reachability.py:152-210`). It yields body INDICES rather than lines, because the body's matchers do NOT share a projection -- and that split is the load-bearing part, not the adoption itself.
      - ITEMS read `views` with blockquoted lines skipped; the three stamp regexes read PHYSICAL lines. Measured 2026-08-12: `views` keeps the blockquote marker only on the line that OPENS the quote, so in an ordinary two-line stamp block the first line still matches and the quality-review continuation under it does not -- a fully stamped section would read as verified-but-unreviewed and silently stop being DONE. Physical also keeps this agreeing with `sequencer_triage.section_stamps()` (`.claude/hooks/sequencer_triage.py:56`), the authority `_is_done` mirrors.
      - Shipped with the shared fixture at `scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py:test_section_context_closure_section47`, whose control proves the physical-line classifier saw only section 1 and swept the nested item into it. Corpus differential 0 across all 281 files.
- [x] `todo-staged-check.py` counts and provenance-checks a nested section
      `_post_image_lines` publishes `(lines, mask, leaves, in_bq, reason)` and the caller builds `sec_vis`, the section-heading view: the leaf projection with fenced AND blockquoted lines blanked, index-parallel to `vis` so every lineno-keyed check keeps one coordinate system (`scripts/todo-staged-check.py:122-159`, `:576`). `_section_total`, `added_sections`, `_park_into_shipping_section` and both provenance checks take it; the blockquote-anchored body matchers keep reading physical lines for the reason above.
      - `added_sections` now classifies from the post-image projection indexed BY LINE NUMBER rather than from the raw diff text, which is what let a nested `- ## N.` enter the graph without ever counting against the cap.
      - The blockquote predicate ships WITH the projection, because publishing one without the other is a regression rather than a partial fix: `leaf_views` turns a quoted `> ## 99.` into a real heading, so quoted examples would count against the cap, be asked for provenance, and one landing between a newly added stamp and a newly added park would SPLIT their bounds and silently suppress the park-into-shipping refusal (Codex design review, §47, [high]).
- [x] `validate.py`'s anchor check stops reporting a false dead anchor
      `_scan_markdown` takes the shared `ScanResult` and matches `_ATX_RE` against `leaf_views`, so a list-contained `- ## 2. Nested` yields its heading and `[jump](#2-nested)` resolves (`scripts/todo-graph/validate.py:1808-1878`). Links still come from the physical line: a container prefix cannot hide one from `_ANCHOR_LINK_RE`, and `_is_escaped` indexes the same string it searched.
      - BLOCKQUOTED HEADINGS ARE KEPT HERE, the one deliberate divergence in the closure. The two graph-context walks drop them because a quoted heading is an example; this check models what GitHub RENDERS, and GitHub gives a heading inside a blockquote a real slug. Both directions are pinned by controls, including one proving a genuinely dead anchor still fails.
- [x] A staged document whose git and scan coordinates disagree is REFUSED
      Filed and fixed from this section's own design review, and it is a defect the adoption would have DEEPENED rather than one it introduced. `_added_lines` numbers lines from `git diff`, which delimits on LF alone, while `scan_text` first maps lone CR / VT / FF / FS / GS / RS / NEL / U+2028 / U+2029 to newline -- so one git line becomes several projection lines and every lineno-keyed check addresses unrelated text. `_park_into_shipping_section` already mixed the two coordinate systems; indexing `added_sections` into the projection made that dependency load-bearing.
      - The end-of-run tree-id re-bind proves both reads saw the same BLOB, never that their coordinate systems agree, so nothing downstream could have caught it. `_ODD_BREAK_RE` refuses through the existing `unscannable` channel. CRLF is deliberately excluded via `\r(?!\n)`: `normalize_newlines` collapses it first and git counts it as one ending, so refusing it would reject every CRLF-authored TODO over a disagreement that does not exist. Measured: 0 of 281 corpus files carry such a break (Codex design review, §47, [medium]).
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: the gate consumers adopt the section-context projections"`

**Test checkpoint:** ONE shared nested-section fixture drives `build.py`, `todo-reachability.py`, `todo-staged-check.py` and `validate.py` to the same section set and the same item ownership, each with a control proving the physical-line classifier disagreed; a genuinely dead anchor still fails the anchor check; the live corpus differential stays 0/281 and the built cache stays byte-identical; `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`, `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Test runner:** `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` (592 assertions; `test_section_context_closure_section47`, `test_staged_coordinate_refusal_section47`, `test_logical_section_addition_section47`, `test_section_identity_shapes_section47`, `test_baseline_fail_closed_section47`) and `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (627/627). Host-side tooling only -- no kernel test surface.

> **Notes:** Three gates -- `todo-reachability.py`, `todo-staged-check.py`, `todo-graph/validate.py` -- now classify sections from the same `leaf_views`/`views` projections `build.py` adopted in §45, ending a producer/enforcement disagreement about what a section is.
> The closure is deliberately NOT uniform in two places, and both are pinned by controls: heading matchers skip blockquoted lines for GRAPH context but keep them for the ANCHOR check (GitHub renders and slugs a quoted heading), and the `> **` stamp matchers stay on physical lines because `views` strips the marker on every continuation line.
> Reachability's stamp grammar was aligned to `sequencer_triage.section_stamps()`, the authority `_is_done` mirrors; the three patterns previously disagreed in both directions on `>**Verified:**` and `> **Verified:`.
> Section identity in the staged gate is occurrence-aware (a `Counter` multiset difference), so a section created by a CONTAINER edit alone is caught while a renumber, move or delete-and-re-add is not mistaken for new work.
> Two memory fixes came out of the perf leg: the body walk yields BOUNDS and streams once (200k-line section: 8 MB of boxed indices -> 2,655 bytes peak), and the staged section view is lazy rather than a dense copy (1,624,176 -> 62,807 bytes, 25.9x).
> Scope boundary: `.claude/hooks/sequencer_triage.py` carries the same nested-heading blindness and is CONTROL PLANE the unattended run may not edit -- filed, not fixed.

## 48. The Mutating Repair Tool Stops Rewriting the Raw HTML It Is Shown

> **Spawned-by:** §47 (split)
> **User impact:** `python3 scripts/todo-reflow.py --write` silently edits the literal bytes of a raw-HTML block in a TODO -- it joins a long `<details ...>` opening tag onto the paragraph below it -- so an author running the repair tool that `validate-todo-file` step 2 wires in gets an HTML block they did not write, from a tool whose contract is to move line breaks and nothing else.

Split out of §47 on 2026-08-12, before any code, on `section-manifest.py`'s `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (5 subsystems)` verdict. The seam is the one this file has used twice already (§38/§41, §42/§43) and it is an acceptance boundary rather than an administrative one: §47's three adopters are GATES, proven right when their blocking VERDICT is unchanged across the corpus, while this one is a MUTATING tool, proven right when the corpus is BYTE-IDENTICAL after it runs under `--diff`. No file is touched by both sections. Reviewing them together would put a verdict-parity argument and a byte-parity argument under one adversarial round.

> **Split override (structured waiver, accepted 2026-08-12):** this section scores `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED (4 subsystems)` while carrying exactly ONE work item, so there is nothing to split and the verdict is overridden on that ground alone. Estimate: 3 files (1 production -- `scripts/todo-reflow.py`, plus `cache_schema.py` only if the retirement route wins; 2 test), 1 real subsystem, ~3 tests, ~80K context. All four counted subsystems but one are the shared test surface (`scripts/tests`, `scripts/todo-graph`, `scripts/test-tooling.sh`) that every section of this file touches. Recorded rather than silently waived because a one-item section tripping the predictor is evidence about the PREDICTOR, filed as such.

**Continuation waiver (inherits §47's review depth 4, limit 3, accepted 2026-08-12).** `user_impact`: as above -- a repair tool that edits raw HTML the author hand-wrote. `not_parkable`: §47 is the parent and is stamped when it ships, and the fixpoint loop never revisits a DONE section, so a `- [/]` park there strands the item with no owner; §42, whose `PROSE_VISIBLE_KINDS` seam this either fixes or retires, is stamped and closed. `severity_trend`: bounded and NARROWING -- the type-7 form destroyed the block and republished hidden structure as graph data, and §46 fixed it; this type-6 residue moves literal bytes only, which is why it is a smaller separate section rather than a continuation at the same severity. `surface`: `scripts/todo-reflow.py`, plus `PROSE_VISIBLE_KINDS` in `scripts/todo-graph/cache_schema.py` if the retirement route is taken.

- [x] `todo-reflow.py` REWRITES literal raw-HTML content inside a type-6 block, joining a long opener into the prose after it
      MEASURED 2026-08-12 while wiring §46's type-7 row into the same projection. `todo-reflow.py` takes `.prose_mask()`, which deliberately shows type-6 blocks, and its hard-wrap detector treats any line of 78-138 characters as a candidate wrapped line -- including the block's OPENING TAG. With `<details data-x="` + 76 chars + `">` followed by three ~90-character prose lines, the tool joins the opener onto the paragraph and rewrites the document.
      - What it does NOT do, and this is why the severity is bounded: type 6's opener is NAME-anchored (`^</?(names)(?=(\s|/?>|$))`) so `<details ...> prose...` still opens a type-6 block, and the `## 99.` heading and `- [x]` item inside it stay masked. Verified after the rewrite -- kind is still `tag-block` and both fake lines are still hidden. So this moves literal bytes rather than exposing structure.
      - THE TYPE-7 FORM OF THIS WAS NOT DEFERRED -- it was FIXED in §46 by removing `complete-tag` from `PROSE_VISIBLE_KINDS`, because type 7 requires a COMPLETE TAG ALONE ON THE LINE and the same join DESTROYS the block, republishing the heading and item as graph data. §46 carries the regression test and the control.
      - An earlier version of this item claimed reflow could never reflow such a body at all. That was measured on a 15-character opener that can never enter the 78-138 band, so the conclusion was fixture-specific and false; it is corrected here rather than left standing.
      - SHIPPED AS A BOUNDARY, NOT A HIDE. `ScanResult.prose_html_boundary_mask()` says which lines of a block `prose_mask` SHOWS are the author's literal tag bytes (`scripts/todo-graph/cache_schema.py:2261`), and `reflow()` ends its run on them (`scripts/todo-reflow.py:220`). Type 6 stays prose-visible, so the 30 live `<details>` lines are still linted; the prose BETWEEN the tags still joins, which is the control that stops "protect it" from degrading into "stop reflowing".
      - THE OPENER-ONLY ACCEPTANCE THIS ITEM ORIGINALLY NAMED WAS INSUFFICIENT, measured before implementing and again in the design review. Protecting only the opener leaves `</details>` as the run's LAST line, and `_hard_wrapped` width-checks `block[:-1]` only, so the closer is joined onto the prose instead -- the fix would have moved the defect rather than closed it.
      - AND IT WOULD HAVE ADDED A NEW ONE. A type-6 opener is NAME-anchored, so `<details` alone on a line opens the block; a line-local "starts with `<`" rule copies that through and then presents an in-band `data-x="...">` continuation as the first line of a fresh run, joining it into the paragraph. The current tool does NOT make that rewrite (the short `<details` line fails the 78-column check), so the naive fix introduces a corruption path (Codex design review, §48, [high]; reproduced both before and after).
      - The boundary is therefore STATEFUL and quote-aware: a tag still open at end of line carries to the next, so a tag split across a line ending is protected in both directions. Tag starts are recognised through the existing grammar's character classification, not `str.isalpha()` -- 24 of the 30 live type-6 lines are ordinary prose and several carry `>=`, so a rule written over the characters `<`/`>` would freeze exactly the prose the seam exists to lint.
      - EACH CONSTRUCT ENDS AT ITS OWN TERMINATOR, never at the first `>`. A generic `>` closes `<!-- a > b -->`, `<?php $a > $b ?>` and `<![CDATA[a > b]]>` while each is still open, so the carry dropped early and the remainder was joined. The CDATA document was byte-identical BEFORE this projection existed, which made the generic terminator a REGRESSION rather than a gap -- caught and fixed in-section (Codex adversarial, §48, [medium]).
      - A NESTED RAW-TEXT ELEMENT'S CONTENT IS NEVER PROSE. Inside an already-open type-6 block the scanner does not re-open a block for `<script>`/`<style>`/`<textarea>`/`<pre>`, so nothing marked their bodies: three 90-column script lines joined, putting the first line's `//` comment in front of every statement after it. This one is PRE-EXISTING, not a regression -- measured, the pre-change tool produced byte-identical output on the same fixture, because the indent rule already split that run (Codex adversarial, §48, [high]; the "left intact" half of the claim was rejected on that measurement).
      - Acceptance MET on the shapes above plus a blockquoted `> <details>` seen as markup through `leaf_views`, each paired with the prose still joining, and each with a wrong-mask control that brings the corruption back so no assertion can pass for an unrelated reason. Negative side pinned too: `a < b`, `<élan` and `>= 30 min` are NOT markup and still join.
      - THE POST-SHIP REVIEW RAN 8 ROUNDS AND EVERY ROUND BUT THE LAST FOUND A REAL DEFECT, most of them REGRESSIONS THIS SECTION'S OWN FIX HAD INTRODUCED: a generic `>` terminator, a first-match-wins raw scan cancelled by another element's closer, a `pending` opener lost at the line boundary, a prefix-only closer accepting `</script bogus>`, a quadratic `line[i:]` tail copy, and a fast-path lookahead that excluded `<script>` but not `</script>`. Each was reproduced before the fix and re-measured after. Round 7 found no production defect; round 8's was fixed in one token.
      - Two REJECTIONS on measurement, both recorded because the reviewer's reasoning was sound and its premise was not: the nested-`<script>` join was called a regression, and the pre-change tool produced byte-identical output on that fixture (the indent rule already split the run), so it is PRE-EXISTING and was fixed here anyway; and a dense-`<a>` case read as superlinear in-suite while the same regex is ~linear in isolation.
      - ACCEPTED RESIDUE, measured not assumed: 16 MiB of `<a b="c">` on ONE line inside a type-6 block costs 4.59s, because quoted attributes are deliberately outside the C-level fast path. It is LINEAR, and a quote-aware fast-path regex risks catastrophic backtracking -- a worse failure mode than the cost. No user reaches this shape; it is recorded rather than chased.
- [x] A document whose line breaks and mask coordinates disagree is REFUSED
      Same class §47 refused in `todo-staged-check.py`, and the adoption made it load-bearing here: `reflow()` numbers lines with `split("\n")` while its masks come from a scan that also folds VT, FF, FS, GS, RS, NEL and U+2028/9, so every mask index shifts and a masked region is under-protected at its leading edge. `_norm()` cannot catch it because joining preserves content whichever lines were joined. Refused through the existing exit-2 contract (`scripts/todo-reflow.py:301`).
      - THE `\r` HALF IS INERT IN THIS TOOL, unlike in §47, and the fixture says so rather than copying the rationale. `process()` reads in TEXT mode, so universal newlines turn a lone CR into `\n` before either coordinate system sees it -- measured: on-disk `a\rb` reads back as `a\nb`, both counts 3. The test uses VT, which survives the read; a CR fixture would exit 0 and prove nothing. §47's gate reads through git, which performs no such translation.
      -> XREF: [`TODO-06 §46`](#46-commonmark-html-block-type-7-needs-a-non-backtracking-matcher) -- the section that measured this and fixed the type-7 half of it (item: "Recognise CommonMark HTML block type 7, which §42 withdrew rather than ship").
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: the mutating repair tool stops rewriting raw HTML"`

**Test checkpoint:** `python3 scripts/todo-reflow.py --diff` over the tool's own 281-file target set produces BYTE-IDENTICAL output before and after the fix; every raw-HTML line of the four tag fixtures survives verbatim, with a control proving ordinary wrapped prose in the same document still reflows (so the fix did not simply disable reflow); `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` and `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green.

> **Checkpoint corrected on measurement (2026-08-12).** This block previously demanded ZERO changed lines from `--diff` over the corpus, before and after. That is false about the corpus and always was: the 281 files carry 877 diff lines of genuine hard-wrapped prose, which is the standing population `scripts/lint.sh` Check 19 WARNs on and the repair this tool exists to offer. A run asserting zero would have failed on a healthy tree. The invariant that actually proves this fix moved nothing is BYTE-IDENTICAL diff output against the pre-fix tool, which is what was measured (877 lines both sides, `diff` clean).

> **Test runner:** `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` (707 assertions at close-out 2026-08-13, 706 at this section's own ship via `test_raw_html_is_not_rewritten_section48`; the 707th is the orphan-check scan-cost regression the close-out sweep added) and `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (627/627). Host-side tooling only -- no kernel test surface.

> **Notes:** `todo-reflow.py` no longer moves the literal tag bytes of a CommonMark type-6 block: `prose_html_boundary_mask()` marks the markup lines and the reflow run ends on them, while the prose between them still joins.
> Type 6 stays in `PROSE_VISIBLE_KINDS` -- the retirement route was rejected because it would discard the 30 live `<details>` prose lines that are the only reason §42 opened the seam.
> The boundary carries per-CONSTRUCT terminators (`-->`, `?>`, `]]>`, quote-aware `>`) so a construct split across a line ending is protected in both directions, and a nested `<script>`/`<style>`/`<textarea>`/`<pre>` body is held verbatim -- closing a pre-existing join that put a `//` comment in front of the statements after it.
> Performance is asserted as SCALING across a 4x size step in a FRESH SUBPROCESS: in-process, building several 16 MiB payloads made the allocator the thing being measured, and both an absolute bound and a ratio read correct code as defective.
> A document whose `split("\n")` and mask coordinates disagree is refused through the exit-2 contract (the §47 class; its CR half is inert here because `process()` reads in text mode), and the corpus differential is byte-identical before and after at 877 pre-existing hard-wrap lines.
> Scope boundary: `format-md-tables.py` consumes the STRUCTURAL mask and never sees type 6, so it is unaffected and was not touched.

> **Verified:** 2026-08-13 | commit `ac0255808` + review fixes | 3/3 items | build OK | corpus differential byte-identical to the pre-fix tool (877 lines both sides), test_todo_fence 706 assertions, test_build 627/627, test-tooling 1323/1323, lint 0 errors
> **Quality reviewed:** 2026-08-13 | Codex 12x (design, adversarial, test-coverage, consistency, perf, re-adversarial x7) | 7H+8M fixed, 0 open | scope: N/A (host-side Python tooling; no kernel/boot domain skill applies)

-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §47`](#47-the-gate-consumers-adopt-the-section-context-projections-the-producer-already-uses) -- the parent this was split out of, which owns the three GATE adopters of the same projections (item: "Commit: `\"todo-graph: the gate consumers adopt the section-context projections\"`").

> **After §1-§3:** Impossible OS has full Linux-parity ownership metadata plus the dep-graph that neither OS ships, plus automated cross-file XREF integrity checks AND a CI-gated JSON Schema sidecar (consumed by `remark-lint-frontmatter-schema`; no Linux equivalent for project plans). Editor-time diagnostics are best-effort developer convenience via `markdown-yaml-embedded-langservers`; the §6 CI gate is the authoritative line of defense.
> **After §4-§6:** "what should I work on next?" + "what's most-blocking?" + "what's been stale for 90 days?" are one-command queries, and graph drift is caught at PR time instead of at next-reviewer-sweep time, with `--diff` surfacing graph regressions per-PR. The canonical-markdown / derived-cache invariant matches the existing [Hook Routing Matrix](../../docs/infrastructure/ai-system.md#hook-routing-matrix) architecture, so contributors already understand the mental model.
> **After §7:** the dependency graph becomes a clickable mermaid diagram in `docs/infrastructure/todo-graph.md` rendering inline on GitHub with status colors and direct links into source files. Five output formats (mermaid / graphviz / ASCII / Gantt / markdown table) cover terminal users, CI artifact viewers, PR descriptions, and external dashboards alike.
> **After §14-§18:** the resolution rule is not merely shared but PROVEN shared end to end: §16 made the verdict differential run itself, §17 gave both readers one cache-schema validator, and §18 closes the three things that gate still could not see -- the producer that feeds it, the lint consumer beside it, and its own inability to tell a vocabulary migration from a behaviour change. The gate also now bounds its own runtime rather than discovering the CI ceiling as a timeout.
> **After §19:** trusting the cache is a per-CALLER declaration rather than one fixed contract: a reader states which subtrees it consumes separately from which readiness conditions it requires, so routing a new reader no longer means refusing a cache that is schema-valid and entirely usable to it. Two more readers are routed, each refusing an untrustworthy cache with its OWN infrastructure exit code distinct from its verdict codes -- the separation that keeps "the audit could not run" from reading as "the audit found nothing". Neither Win11 nor Linux has an equivalent; the closest analogue is per-tool ad hoc parsing where every consumer re-derives its own notion of a usable index.
> **After §20:** the vocabulary a resolver can emit is a DECLARATION rather than an inference from its source text, so retiring a bucket is provable instead of permanently refused. §18 could only ask whether a name still appeared in the emitters, which an indexed or concatenated emission defeats, so it closed the retirement path rather than approve one on unsound evidence; §20 replaces the evidence with `EMITTED_BUCKETS` -- an AST-pinned declaration backed by a runtime bound in both outcome constructors -- and the identity gate now approves the clearing path it had to refuse. Neither Win11 nor Linux has an equivalent: the closest analogue is a deprecation comment plus a grep, which is precisely the evidence this replaces.
> **After §21:** freshness stops being a proxy. Both readers asked "is the cache newer than the corpus?", which is an ordering of two wall-clock stamps -- so a backward clock step made a correct cache look stale (one identity-gate fixture failed roughly 1 run in 4 on an unchanged tree), and, worse, the PRODUCER could publish a cache that was legitimately newer than a TODO it had already misread. §21 replaces the proxy with the question itself: the producer fingerprints the bytes it actually parsed, re-verifies them before any write, and refuses at a distinct exit code with the previous cache left byte-identical; readers compare that recorded fingerprint, plus the git history the derived timestamps came from, against what is on disk. Neither Win11 nor Linux has an equivalent -- build systems and index tools almost universally decide staleness by timestamp comparison, which is the class of check this removes rather than tunes.
> **After §22:** the widest reader stops answering from a partial cache. `query.py` dropped malformed rows silently, so `stats` could report a corpus of two after discarding five, and a scalar `depends_on` -- flattened to `[]` by a defensive `_safe_list` -- made the unmet-dependency loop iterate zero times and report a BLOCKED TODO as ready. It is now routed through the shared validator under a profile declaring all four subtrees it consumes, refusing at rc 2 with a machine-readable body, and the refusal survives the surfaces in front of it: the MCP tool call returns a structured error instead of the `[]` an agent could not distinguish from "nothing matched", and watch mode refuses the tick rather than serving a graph that predates the edit which broke the build. Freshness binds to the repo's canonical cache, so an explicitly-supplied `--cache` is still shape-checked without pretending a foreign corpus was certified. The comparison is not that Win11 or Linux does this worse but that the failure is normally invisible: ctags and cscope answer from a stale index without a distinct code, and every consumer re-derives its own notion of "usable enough". The review then found that the refusal was honest in the BODY and dishonest in every other observable: the MCP handler reported `isError: false` on it, and ten command handlers returned the cache-unusable code for an ordinary bad argument with no body at all -- so a caller was told to rebuild a cache that was fine. Request errors now have their own code and envelope, are held until after the post-walk generation check so a corpus edit landing mid-walk refuses as stale rather than blaming the request, and -- being raised rather than exited -- no longer kill a watcher when the watched target is renamed.
> **After §24:** the freshness question gets a PRICE, and the price is charged per command. §21 established that a cache is not a pure function of the TODO bytes -- `created_at` / `last_active_at` come from the corpus git log, so identical bytes over a rewritten history are stale -- but establishing that costs a full path-limited history walk, measured here at 47.9ms against a 169ms query invocation and growing with TOTAL repo commits rather than the corpus-touching ones §21 assumed. The cheap substitute was evaluated and REJECTED with a reproduced counterexample: a digest of git's shallow-boundary file misses a `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE` override that the commit count catches, because the count is computed by git under the same environment as the walk. So the identity stands and the AXIS moved instead: three of thirteen commands actually dereference a git-derived timestamp, and only those three declare `requires_history`. The comparison is not that Win11 or Linux decides this worse but that neither poses the question -- an index tool rebuilds wholesale or not at all, with no notion that one query needs a stricter freshness guarantee than its neighbour.
> **After §25:** the cache stops being an artifact you have to take on faith. Three fields the readers consumed had no producer behind them, and each degraded silently rather than failing: `render.py` read an `effort` the producer could not emit, so every Gantt row was `1w` and the schema would have rejected a contributor who tried to author one; `ready` / `blocked` / `blocking` ranked against a file-level `depends_on` that zero of 232 TODOs author, so `blocking` -- advertised in this very table as the critical-path rank -- returned empty for every possible corpus; and nothing identified the cache's own contract, so `validate.py --diff` validated a historical baseline on SHAPE alone and could only ASK the caller to regenerate it. `effort` now runs end to end behind one grammar that REFUSES a bad value instead of defaulting (the first draft accepted `2w\n`, because Python's `$` matches before a trailing newline -- caught by the probe's own control, not by review). The readiness verbs keep their single source by decision and now NAME the empty edge set, because the richer section-level evidence is not a drop-in: 1,806 of its 2,529 groups target `self`, and 26 of its 145 cross-file targets are bare stems matching up to 18 files each, which is an unresolved-reference problem §26 owns rather than a source swap. And the artifact now declares the producer contract that wrote it -- a hand-bumped version for a meaning change, a digest over the DECLARED field set AND each field's emission mode for a structural one, with the producer refusing to emit any key it has not declared and refusing to IMPORT when its own declarations disagree. Review found that same class one layer down, twice, and both are closed: the two published JSON Schemas carried the very `$`-anchored pattern the Python grammar had already rejected, so `jsonschema` accepted `2w\n` where the producer refused it -- the fixture missed it by hand-reimplementing the validator instead of calling it; and the contract was enforced in ONE direction only, so a field that stopped being emitted, or was reclassified from always-emitted to conditional, still published under an unchanged identity. Neither Win11 nor Linux has an equivalent: an index either rebuilds wholesale or answers from whatever it finds, with no notion that an artifact should be able to say which tool made it.

> **After §8:** Claude Code, Cursor, and Aider call `ready` / `blocked` / `backlinks` / `code` directly via the MCP `todo-graph` server (~150 LOC over §4). Read-only by design; write operations stay in the markdown files. Closes the AI-agent-developer-experience axis where Win11 (closed) and Linux (none in-tree) both score ❌. taskmd 2026 ships `taskmd mcp start` for the same purpose; matching that surface keeps Impossible OS at parity with the 2026 standard.

## Unit Tests

> Host-side tooling -- no kernel `test_runner_init()` wiring. Tests are bash + python scripts co-located with the generator/validator under `scripts/todo-graph/tests/`.

- [x] Consolidated all planned test coverage into [`scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`](../../scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh) (single-file harness, stdlib bash)
      - The plan's five separate files became ONE runner because every test shares the same mktemp + `t_pass`/`t_fail` + fixture-tree helpers; splitting them would have duplicated ~200 LOC of plumbing.
      - It carried 105/105 sub-tests when §1-§8 wired it and 627/627 at close-out 2026-08-13, covering all five planned surfaces (mapping below).
      - Running it a second time produces a byte-identical cache, and the node count matches `find todo -name 'TODO-*.md' -not -name 'TODO-00-INDEX.md' | wc -l` (sub-tests 1-4).
- [x] Validator coverage (originally `test_validate.sh` plan) lives in sub-tests 8a-8m: clean fixture passes all 8 checks (0 failures); stale-XREF fires on a broken `depends_on`; dangling `§N` + cycle detection via DFS (named on both ids); bat-alignment fires on a missing per-category run-*.bat; status-transition fires when `status: done` has an unchecked IO row; `$schema` reachability rejects non-existent + repo-escape paths; `--fix-line-numbers` dry-run preserves content, `--write` rewrites, ambiguity refuses with exit 1; `--diff <baseline>` reports added orphans + broken-backlink (with referrer source) + status-downgrade + newly-stale-xref. New 8th check `duplicate-id` (Codex pass 15 H2) asserts global id uniqueness.
- [x] Query coverage (originally `test_query.sh` plan) lives in sub-tests 9a-9cc: `ready`/`blocked`/`blocking` behavior gated on the pre-migration notice; `backlinks` round-trip via slug + filename stem + nonexistent-hint; `deferred`/`deferred-by` self-pointing filter; `stale --days 0` returns all, `--days 1000000` returns none; `stats --json` emits all required keys; `code`/`code-by` grep surfaces; `orphans` non-empty subset; `--format markdown` emits valid GFM header+separator. Hardening regressions cover cache file-path boundary, ambiguous slug, ambiguous stem, TSV control-char sanitization, malformed top-level + nested-None + scalar collection fields, mixed-shape cache detection, watch-loop reload.
- [x] Render coverage (originally `test_render.sh` plan) lives in sub-tests 12a-12j: mermaid `flowchart TD` + status classDefs + per-node `click` directives; dot digraph with `rankdir=LR` + shape declarations; ascii tree rooted at supplied id + cycle detection + diamond-DAG `(seen)` vs `(cycle)` marker; gantt header + `dateFormat YYYY-MM-DD` + section grouping; markdown GFM table; `--scope` strict subset; `--output` file write + stdout suppression; md5-hash-suffixed mermaid ids prevent sanitize-equal collisions (Codex pass 16 H1); `make todo-graph-render-mermaid` wraps in a ```mermaid fence.
- [x] Schema coverage (originally `test_schema.sh` plan) lives in sub-tests 6d + 6e + 6j + 6k: JSON Schema Draft 2020-12 validates cleanly; 10 negative cases reject (uppercase id, trailing dash, leading digit, unknown status, missing title, string `schema_version`, future `schema_version`, uppercase `depends_on` element, hand-authored `created_at`, hand-authored `last_active_at`); generator forbidden-field FATAL on hand-authored cache-only fields; schema/generator parity on `schema_version<1` + empty title + non-string `$schema` + duplicate `depends_on`.
- [x] Wired tests into [`scripts/test-tooling.sh`](../../scripts/test-tooling.sh) at the `[todo-graph]` block (lines ~529-544). The aggregate runner now captures the dynamic `[test_build] N/N passed` summary instead of hard-coding a sub-test count, so the message tracks current state without manual refresh.
- [x] Commit: test additions landed incrementally with each of §1-§8's implementation commits (22, 37, 54, 66, 77, 88, 100, 104, 105 sub-tests across the roll-out). No single `test/todo-graph: add ...` commit because each section shipped its own slice of the surface.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (group: `[todo-graph]`) | expected: all `t_pass`, 0 `t_fail`. Current at close-out 2026-08-13: **627/627** sub-tests PASS on the single consolidated `scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` runner (105/105 when §1-§8 wired it; the other 522 arrived with §9-§48). The companion `python3 scripts/tests/test_todo_fence.py` reports 707 assertions. For host-side tests this replaces the kernel `scripts/debug/kernel/run-<cat>-tests.bat` pattern; the test surface is shell/python, not kernel C.

On a tree with `draft` nodes, the subcommand returns those with satisfied deps -- covered by regression sub-test 9m (synthetic fixture: `status=done` foundation + `status=draft` consumer -> `ready` prints the consumer).

- [x] `python3 scripts/todo-graph/query.py stats --format markdown` produces a GFM table with `## TODO graph stats` header + By status / By domain / Top blocking / Top longest-deferred sub-sections. Verified 2026-04-23.

Empty output because every TODO's `last_active_at` is younger than 90 days (fresh migration landed today). `last_active_at` is derived from `git log` per §1's cache-only auto-derived field contract. Covered by regression 9j/9k (`--days 0` returns every node, `--days 1000000` returns none).
- [x] `python3 scripts/todo-graph/query.py code todo-metadata-layer` grep surface exits 0. Verified 2026-08-13 at close-out: exit 0, and it now returns a row (`src/kernel/foo.c  notes-grep`) rather than the empty output this line was `[/]` for.
    - The row is real, not a placeholder leak: it is the `(src/kernel/foo.c)` reference in this file's own regression-9p prose, which is exactly the shape the `..`-filter is meant to keep. The subcommand's live behaviour and its synthetic regression now agree on the same corpus.
- [x] `make todo-graph-render-mermaid` regenerates [`docs/infrastructure/todo-graph.md`](../../docs/infrastructure/todo-graph.md) with `# TODO Dependency Graph` header + `mermaid` fence + 200-node flowchart + closing fence. GitHub renders inline. Regenerated 2026-04-23 as part of this close-out.
- [/] `python3 scripts/todo-graph/query.py render --format dot | dot -Tsvg > /tmp/g.svg` -- operator-gated, graphviz absent on this host
    - The `render --format dot` output itself is verified in regression 12c; a host with `dot` installed gets the SVG. Skipped per the spec's "skipped if graphviz absent" exemption.
    - `command -v dot` re-checked at close-out 2026-08-13 and still absent, so the exemption is still live rather than having gone unexamined.

- [x] PostToolUse auto-rewrite hook fires on TODO edits. Demonstrated 2026-04-23 during §6 review-todo-section run: edits to TODO-06's stamps triggered the hook, which rewrote stale `(item: "..." at line N)` parentheticals in TODO-03 (line 128 -> 136), TODO-04 (line 110 -> 508, line 213 -> 221), and TODO-05 (line 38 -> 46). Those rewrites landed in §6's commit `195b3d92` as evidence. Hook lives in [`.claude/settings.json`](../../.claude/settings.json) on the Edit|Write|MultiEdit matcher.

The "introduces regressions" half is covered by regression sub-tests 11g2 (typo-id correction -> no false positive), 11g3 (removed id with live referrer -> broken-backlink), and 11h (status-downgrade done->active -> detected).

The stock Red Hat YAML extension alone does NOT auto-detect `$schema:` in markdown frontmatter. (manual -- requires a VS Code session + extension install.)
